## Optimizer Papers

### 2026

#### October 2026

| Date | Paper Title | Summary |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2026-10-16 | [BMuon: Budgeting Muon's Spectral Geometry for Structured Compressibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23571) | Makes MLP hidden channels compete for a global allocation budget, applying coupled channel weights inside and outside Muon's polar maps. A minorize-maximize allocation update uses channel scores and scalar bisection; refreshing every five steps costs 1.2 polar evaluations per budgeted matrix on average. Three-seed vision experiments improve gradual-pruning accuracy. A 1B LLaMA-style model trained on 13.1B FineWeb tokens retains perplexity 12.85 after 80% channel pruning, but dense perplexity is worse than Muon's 10.92. Representation-cost guarantees concern linear factorizations, not training implicit bias. The official repository is currently a README-only release placeholder, so no implementation link is listed. |
| 2026-10-15 | [A Second-Order Descent Algorithm for Nonlinear Equality Constrained Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21673) | Combines sequential quadratic programming with homogeneous second-order descent for nonlinear equality constraints. Each trial splits into a normal feasibility step and a reduced-Hessian tangential step obtained from a generalized homogeneous eigenvalue model; merit-ratio acceptance adapts regularization and handles negative-curvature hard cases. Its epsilon-to-the-minus-three-halves iteration claim requires the explicit structural condition kappa_h=O(1), alongside smoothness and constraint assumptions. Deterministic MATLAB experiments report 81 solved CUTEst problems and favorable evaluation counts against SQP-type baselines. Competitor runtimes are not compared, and Hessians, null-space construction, and eigenvalue solves remain substantial costs; these results do not establish scalable stochastic neural-network training performance. |
| 2026-10-15 | [When is a better LLM worth paying for? Stochastic Optimization with Switching Mixed-Fidelity Oracles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21139) | Introduces cost-aware stochastic-optimization wrappers that switch among biased, mixed-fidelity gradient oracles. With known bias, restarted stages choose the cheapest admissible contraction, using biased stochastic gradient extrapolation as one realization; with unknown bias, sparse trusted-gradient certification rejects unsuccessful sources. Guarantees assume smooth strongly convex objectives, state-dependent noise, and valid oracle admissibility bounds. Synthetic regression and LLM-based missing-covariate completion illustrate lower equivalent information cost than fixed-fidelity choices. Experiments use clean-Hessian whitening and a practical bias proxy rather than the sufficient worst-case rule; completion prices are amortized accounting costs, not measured end-to-end neural-model training speedups. No substantive official implementation was located. |
| 2026-10-14 | [Envelope Cubic Regularized Newton Method for Nonconvex-Strongly-Concave Minimax Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20345) | ECRN applies inexact cubic regularized Newton to a regularized primal envelope and uses a NewtonTrack inner solver to approximate its derivatives. A sharper envelope Hessian-Lipschitz estimate improves condition-number dependence to kappa-to-the-three-quarters in second-order oracle complexity, matching a deterministic lower bound up to logarithmic factors. Robust logistic-regression experiments on three LIBSVM datasets compare gradient norms, oracle calls, and CPU time at condition numbers 100 and 1000. The method requires exact Hessian access to the original objective, nested saddle solves, and prescribed accuracy and regularization. These structured minimax guarantees are not evidence of scalable stochastic or billion-parameter training superiority. |
| 2026-10-14 | [Memory-Efficient Subspace Optimization with Residual Correction: From Momentum SGD to Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19844) | Maintains momentum in a low-dimensional gradient subspace while adding a state-free orthogonal residual, yielding SMRC for momentum SGD and PMRC for Muon. PMRC polar-normalizes projected momentum, then combines its lifted direction with a scaled residual gradient; periodically refreshed bases track changing gradients. Under standard stochastic assumptions, single- and multi-stage variants achieve O(T^-1/4) stationarity rates. On C4 LLaMA-1.3B, PMRC validation loss is 2.570 versus GUM 2.656 and full Muon 2.495; Qwen3-8B IFEval instruction accuracy reaches 69.06%. Practical experiments add residual clipping and approximate SVD, and select hyperparameters using downstream evaluation benchmarks, limiting clean generalization claims. |
| 2026-10-14 | [One Size Does Not Fit All: Curvature-Aware Adaptive Perturbation for SAM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20415) | Adapts SAM's perturbation radius using the ratio of consecutive gradient norms: multiply or divide by a growth factor, then clip to bounds. This reuses SAM's gradients rather than computing Hessian curvature or adding backward passes. Three-run CIFAR and ImageNet experiments report aggregate accuracy gains of 1.14 percentage points on CIFAR-100 and 0.31 points across ImageNet evaluations, with benefits across several SAM variants. The nonconvex convergence analysis additionally requires a decaying-radius envelope and decreasing learning rate; it does not directly certify the bounded experimental controller. Initial radius and growth factor still require tuning, and experiments do not establish language-model benefits. |
| 2026-10-14 | [Recursive Gradient Retraction Methods for Stochastic Nonconvex Optimization on Stiefel Manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19944) | RGR constructs recursive stochastic estimators in ambient Euclidean space, maps them to the Stiefel tangent space, and retracts the update, avoiding explicit vector transport. SPIDER, stochastic recursive momentum, and EMA variants trade estimator accuracy against gradient work; adaptive steps avoid needing a supplied Lipschitz constant, with horizon-free guarantees for the online variants. Experiments evaluate PCA, low-rank completion, and orthogonal CNNs on real datasets and small vision tasks. The methods still incur tangent projection and retraction costs, while SPIDER uses periodic full-gradient refreshes. Baselines have different retraction and tuning choices, so reported gains do not isolate estimator construction in every comparison. |
| 2026-10-13 | [Composite and Stochastic Smoothed Decentralized Bregman Proximal-Gradient Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18231) [Code](https://github.com/shengze-xu/composite-stochastic-sdbpg) | This extension adds node-specific convex nonsmooth regularizers and stochastic local gradients to Bregman primal-dual smoothing with edge-based consensus. It isolates temporal subgradient motion and aggregation mismatch as defect sequences, obtaining an inverse-iteration best squared proximal-residual bound under finite accumulated defects and summable stochastic variance. Imaging, sparse recovery, and phase-retrieval experiments compare reconstruction and communication costs. Fourier imaging reaches a common target with fewer rounds than gradient-tracking baselines, but stochastic-recovery baselines reach their relaxed target earlier. Compact invariant-domain assumptions and active-structure conditions restrict guarantees; reduced communication does not imply universally lower wall-clock time or better reconstruction. |
| 2026-10-13 | [First-order Optimization Methods with Generalized Smoothness for Low-rank Matrix Factorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19059) | This work derives generalized-smoothness step rules for low-rank symmetric factorization, including adaptive Armijo gradient descent and SafeAdam. SafeAdam accepts an adaptive direction only when a descent-angle certificate holds, otherwise reverting to a normalized gradient; a projected variant handles nonnegativity. Experiments on TDT2, Reuters, and CBCL compare iteration counts, time, and factorization objectives. On CBCL, SafeAdam reaches the reported tolerance in 6764 iterations versus Dyn-NoLips's 9115, but it is slower than simpler adaptive GD on text datasets. Guarantees rely on the factorized problem's generalized smoothness and certified steps, not on unrestricted stochastic Adam convergence or large-language-model training. |
| 2026-10-13 | [How Low Can You Go: Fine-Tuning LLMs with Bayesian Optimization (LlMBO)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18504) | LlMBO combines TinyLoRA's ultra-low-dimensional adapters with trust-region Bayesian optimization, a moment-matched logit observation model for pass counts, and dimension-scaled Gaussian-process priors. Inference-only scoring can optimize greedy-decoding accuracy directly, unlike the evaluated policy-gradient objective. Experiments span ten 1.5B–8B models and 2098 runs, finding competitive gains with at most four trainable scalars and roughly 30% of GRPO's training FLOPs. This is a specialized low-dimensional fine-tuning method, not a general full-parameter optimizer. Search bounds depend on a GRPO-derived displacement budget; performance weakens as dimension grows, and greedy-objective comparisons must be distinguished from matched-temperature comparisons. |
| 2026-10-13 | [NBCLT: Parameter-Agnostic Norm-Ball Clipped Local Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17913) | Introduces federated and decentralized local training with control variates, a one-gradient predict-and-correct recurrence, and Euclidean projections onto configurable norm balls. Spectral blocks clip singular values rather than orthogonalize them; PolyGram-RCClip approximates this projection using an offline Gram-polynomial coefficient table. Under smoothness and bounded gradient variance, parameter-agnostic communication/sample bounds of O(epsilon^-2)/O(epsilon^-4) require local-computation budgets growing with the horizon, not fixed local steps. CIFAR-10 federated ResNet-18 experiments favor tuned NBCLT, but hyperparameters are selected using test accuracy. No large-language-model or decentralized empirical validation is provided, and the fixed-degree projector's accumulated residual is not certified. |
| 2026-10-13 | [Same Function, Different Trajectory: No Standard Optimizer Is Equivariant to Transformer Reparameterization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18085) | Adds a function-preserving rebalancing wrapper to standard optimizers by reciprocally rescaling exact Transformer symmetry pairs: Q/K, V/O, ReLU up/down, or SwiGLU value/output. Hard SAGGS equalizes paired Frobenius norms; Soft SAGGS applies damped corrections to reduce disruption when momentum states remain unchanged. A separate canonical restart jointly transforms weights and Adam moments. In seven-seed 123.8M checkpoint-resume tests, imposed scale imbalance worsens perplexity from 20.84 to 24.62, while either wrapper restores 20.85. Gains mainly demonstrate recovery from deliberate rescaling; unperturbed improvements are small or absent. GELU MLP scaling is excluded because it is not an exact symmetry. Reference code appears in the appendix, without a verified repository. |
| 2026-10-12 | [A Feasible Direction Method with Active-Set Identification for Derivative-Free Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17101) | FDM-AS builds linear objective and constraint models inside a derivative-free trust-region method, using a feasible-direction subproblem to favor feasible trials. An active-set estimate on an expanded neighborhood reduces the constraints entering subproblems, with a lazy-model practical variant reducing evaluation work. The analysis establishes Fritz-John accumulation points, KKT points under constraint qualification, and finite identification under nondegeneracy. Small CUTEst experiments report more feasible trials and better evaluation-budget performance than matched SQP-subproblem variants. A feasible starting point is required; six benchmarks lacking a constructed feasible start were discarded. Practical lazy updates and independently charged constraint oracles limit the scope of cost comparisons. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Efficient Preconditioned Optimization Methods with Frequent Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16792) | This expanded study adds one-sided S-ASGO to Frequent-Directions adaptive preconditioning, retaining accumulated discarded mass as isotropic correction and deriving improved primal-dual and mirror-descent regret bounds. A practical EMA-and-momentum variant uses approximate singular subspaces and RMS-scaled updates. CIFAR experiments and 2400-step GPT-2 pretraining show comparable loss to fuller preconditioners with smaller sketched state; the GPT-2 study spans sequence lengths 256 and 512. Core FTSL methods already appeared at ICML 2025, so the recent contribution is the added algorithms, offline guarantees, and experiments. Sketch size trades quality against cost, and the paper-linked ASGO repository is a baseline rather than verified new-method code. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Matching Complexity for Second-Order Stationary Point with Sample Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16832) | This stochastic optimizer checks estimated curvature before spending work on gradients, taking randomly signed negative-curvature steps when needed and descent steps otherwise. Separate binary-ancestor derivative estimators retain checkpoints and reusable sample handles for gradients and complete Hessians. Truncated difference estimates distinguish predictable drift from martingale motion, yielding near-matching epsilon-to-the-minus-three plus curvature-tolerance-to-the-minus-three derivative-query complexity. The result concerns high-dimensional worst-case second-order stationarity, not a practical large-model speed comparison. Sample-wise gradient and Hessian smoothness, almost-sure derivative-noise bounds, bounded population gradients, and public parameter bounds are assumed; both endpoints of same-sample differences and full-Hessian evaluations are explicitly charged. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Matching Full-Hessian Oracle Complexity for Nonconvex-Strongly-Concave Minimax Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16771) | This minimax construction combines saddle-Moreau-envelope gradient or cubic-regularization steps with certified accelerated reduced-dual solves and warm starts amortized across outer iterations. Schur-complement formulas approximate envelope gradients and Hessians from full-Hessian access to the original objective. The resulting accuracy regimes match randomized lower bounds up to logarithmic factors and initialization costs, improving on first-order complexity where curvature regularity helps. It is an algorithmic oracle-complexity result, not a measured optimizer for large networks. Strong dual concavity, Lipschitz gradients and Hessians, prescribed accuracy, and nested certification are required; matrix inversions, linear solves, and cubic subproblem arithmetic are not represented by derivative-query counts alone. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Matching Randomized Bounds for Finite-Sum Nonconvex-Concave Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16991) | These finite-sum minimax methods regularize the dual variable and combine proximal primal updates with either a persistent gradient estimator and independent primal averaging or accelerated dual proximal solves with explicit primal certificates. Reusing anchor estimates across centers controls finite-sum initialization cost, while smoothing stability translates progress to stationarity of the original value function's Moreau envelope. The resulting query regimes match randomized lower bounds across dual-domain diameters up to stated logarithms. This is theoretical algorithm construction, not measured large-model training. Compact known dual domains, mean-squared averaged smoothness, calibrated regularization, large prescribed inner budgets, and Moreau rather than ordinary gradient stationarity constrain interpretation. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Monarch-Muon: Structure-Aware Muon for Fast and Memory-Efficient Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17429) [Code](https://github.com/brain-lab-research/monarch-muon) | Reparameterizes selected dense weights as products of block-diagonal Monarch factors and applies Muon independently to their smaller trainable blocks, reducing weights, gradients, momentum storage, and orthogonalization cost without imposing low rank. Exact-polar convergence follows a blockwise spectral trust-region interpretation. At fixed two-block structure, validation loss improves from Monarch-AdamW's 3.09 to 3.06 on the 257M reference and from 2.77 to 2.68 on the 1B MoE. Four-block 6.89B-reference systems tests reduce optimizer time from 1288 to 304 ms and peak memory from 68.9 to 52.9 GB. Quality runs are single-seed; large-scale timings change parameterization rather than establish matched-quality speedups. Released code omits MoE pretraining experiments. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Spectral-Driven Adaptive-Rank Optimizer for Large Language Model Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16533) [Code](https://github.com/RUCAIBox/Ares) | Targets reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards by estimating gradient spectral entropy, smoothing the implied rank, and truncating matrix gradients within a preset maximum-rank budget before dense AdamW updates. Randomized block power iteration avoids full SVD; vectors retain AdamW. In 300-step GRPO experiments on Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B, seven-benchmark mean mathematical accuracy exceeds AdamW by 5.0/2.7/2.3 percentage points, with equal-budget learning-rate searches and additional RLVR objectives tested. Dense moment states remain, and no fully compute-matched speedup is established. Gradient truncation does not guarantee a strictly low-rank final adaptive update; the public rank-EMA implementation also differs from the paper's accumulator pseudocode. |
| 2026-10-12 | [StructZO: Structured Zeroth-Order Optimization for Memory-Efficient LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16451) | Constructs low-dimensional search spaces from forward-activation Gram sketches, aggregates multiple zeroth-order perturbation queries in these coordinates, applies reduced-space Muon spectral shaping, and lifts updates back to dense weights. Its estimator analysis trades reduced dimension against activation-subspace projection bias. On limited-data Llama3.1-8B tasks, average score reaches 82.9 versus LOZO 76.3 and first-order Adam 84.1; reported peak memory is 20.80 versus Adam 85.29 GiB. The recipe uses 100 queries for 400 steps rather than 20K single-query updates. Wall-clock totals are extrapolated from short timing samples; experiments focus on GLUE/SuperGLUE-style fine-tuning, without reported multi-seed uncertainty or a verified official repository. |
| 2026-10-12 | [Which Geometry on Which Layer? Measured Sign-Spectral Assignment in Mixed-Optimizer Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17285) | Uses gradient alignments and Hessian-vector products to score sign versus spectral directions, routing embedding/output groups while keeping hidden matrices on Muon. Spectral warmup, EMA smoothing, two confirmations, and one-way locking stabilize the gate. A three-seed 31M GPT-2 comparison improves matched-momentum Scion validation loss from 5.084 to 4.955. However, a separate five-seed follow-up favors static spectral assignments over G-Scion, 4.92 versus 5.45; AdamW-edge/Muon also remains stronger in a practical comparison. Approximately 4% overhead was measured in one configuration. Theory concerns ideal memoryless steps, not trajectory superiority; large-scale routing evidence and submitted implementation completeness remain limited. |
| 2026-10-10 | [Adaptive two-points zero-order accelerated methods for stochastic convex optimization problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15202) | These accelerated zeroth-order methods adapt scalar step sizes, minibatch allocation, and smoothing radii from observed function values, without supplied smoothness or variance constants. Two-point estimators, independent pilot and update batches, and explicit budget accounting support smooth and nonsmooth convex guarantees; optional extensions handle unknown solution distance and bounded evaluation corruption. The construction stores linear-dimensional optimizer state and spends two function values per paired sample, with extra adaptive sampling stages. It is an algorithmic theory contribution, not an LLM fine-tuning comparison. Geometry bounds, oracle moment assumptions, initial scales, and corruption information remain relevant; fixed smoothing or corruption can impose residual error floors. |
| 2026-10-10 | [Multilevel Monte Carlo for Zeroth-Order Stochastic Bilevel Optimization with Inexact Lower Level Solves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15047) | ML-DFO combines Gaussian-smoothed outer updates with a multilevel hierarchy of inexact lower-level solves. Coupled neighboring levels reduce correction variance, allowing many cheap coarse solves and fewer expensive fine ones; the analysis tracks solver bias, level-difference decay, and work separately. Field-of-Experts denoising and sparse-view tomography experiments report comparable reconstruction with reduced cumulative lower-level iterations. This is derivative-free bilevel parameter learning, not a general pretrained-model optimizer. Gains depend on suitable coupling and error-versus-cost decay. Practical experiments modify estimator and lower-solver schedules for efficiency, so they illustrate multilevel behavior rather than directly verify every theoretical asymptotic rate. |
| 2026-10-10 | [Parameter-Free Adaptive Extragradient Methods for Generalized-Smooth Variational Inequalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14839) | PF-AEG combines projected extragradient updates with a three-way stepsize rule controlling gradual recovery, observed local operator variation, and operator magnitude. AEG-VR adds variance reduction and randomized snapshot updates for finite-sum problems. Under generalized smoothness and quasi-sharpness, the analysis gives full-sequence convergence and last-iterate rates, including linear convergence across a broader sharpness range. Synthetic VIs, logistic regression, phase retrieval, and distributionally robust optimization illustrate favorable iteration or reported-sample progress. Parameter-free means unknown problem constants are unnecessary, not that initial stepsizes or mixing settings disappear. Projection and repeated operator evaluations remain costs, and these structured guarantees do not cover unrestricted nonconvex neural objectives. |
| 2026-10-10 | [Zeroth-Order Fine-Tuning of LLMs in Learnable Low-Rank Subspaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15225) | AdaSub-ZO learns layerwise low-rank perturbation bases using forward-only finite differences, stabilizes basis learning with EMA, and orthogonalizes with QR. It maintains momentum in the small coordinate space and transfers it when bases change. Three-run OPT-1.3B experiments with rank 36 raise SST-2 accuracy from MeZO's 89.4% to 92.0%, but WiC and WSC do not improve. Basis learning consumes additional forward queries, so iteration counts alone do not measure efficiency. Full-gradient convergence assumes uniform subspace coverage that the learning procedure does not itself prove, and finite smoothing leaves an error floor. Evidence remains limited to small-task language-model fine-tuning. |
| 2026-10-09 | [Adaptive Variance Reduction without Knowing Oracle Smoothness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13799) | A known-budget method probes two independent gradients, switches from direct normalized updates to normalized momentum when two sampled gradients differ beyond a threshold, then runs a normalized-STORM phase and validates two reservoir-selected candidates. Without a known horizon, parallel variance-reduction and AdaGrad-Norm paths use doubling validation blocks. Without knowing smoothness or variance constants, expected gradient-norm rates are O(T^-1/3) under mean-square smoothness, O(T^-1/4) under ordinary smoothness, and O(T^-1/2) without noise; the anytime stochastic rates add logarithms. Assumptions include lower-bounded smooth objectives and unbiased bounded-variance gradients, with repeated-sample oracle access for variance reduction. Guarantees concern selected candidates, not final-iterate quality; no experiments or official code are provided. |
| 2026-10-09 | [Geometry-Aware Multi-Objective Optimization: Norm-Constrained Oracles with Entropic Weight Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13773) | Combines one norm-constrained oracle on blended task gradients with entropic mirror updates of simplex task weights and optional entropy regularization and weight floors. One persistent blended-momentum buffer gives stochastic O(T^-1/4) Pareto stationarity under bounded-gradient assumptions; the unregularized deterministic rate is O(T^-1/2). Three-seed MultiMNIST accuracy is 90.54% versus linear-scalarized Muon's 87.75%, but near-uniform weighting reaches 91.11%, so gains cannot be assigned to adaptive weights alone. Dense-prediction comparisons use one seed and tune on the reporting split. Practical five-step Newton-Schulz errors are not uniformly certified by the theory, and no official implementation is linked. |
| 2026-10-09 | [Optimal Algorithms for Nonsmooth Nonconvex Stochastic Optimization with Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13787) | This work extends online-to-nonconvex conversion to closed convex constraints using local online learners and randomized segment queries. Projection-based anchored gradient descent and projection-free Frank-Wolfe constructions yield Goldstein-style stationarity guarantees; two-point smoothing supplies zeroth-order counterparts. The first-order query rate matches the unconstrained nonsmooth benchmark up to logarithmic factors, while zeroth-order access introduces dimension dependence. The contribution is a theoretical constrained algorithm family rather than an experimental AdamW replacement. Stationarity notions account for boundaries and differ from ordinary gradient norms; projections or linear-minimization calls, local learner geometry, Lipschitz assumptions, and smoothing bias must be included when interpreting practicality and cost. |
| 2026-10-09 | [Partially Informed Stochastic Optimization via Zeroth-Order Residual Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13871) | Piso uses known partial-gradient information and tracks only the residual needed to recover the full gradient. Each two-point probe is centered on the current residual estimate, so sketch variance depends on tracking error rather than reconstructing the unknown component from scratch; Piso2 adds known-component averaging and estimator mixing. Experiments span pricing, strategic classification, performative RL, routing, and security games. A step uses two function-query batches and known-component samples, not free gradients. Guarantees require smoothness and independent probe conditions; an experimentally tested cycling orthonormal-frame variant falls outside the stated independence assumptions, and privileged-response-gradient baselines access additional information. |
| 2026-10-09 | [Randomized Momentum Transport with Cheap Predictors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13789) | Queries a gradient-difference or Hessian-vector momentum correction only with probability q, importance-weighting its residual around a cheap predictor to preserve the conditional mean. Smoothness, transport-bias and residual-control assumptions yield O(T^-1/3) stationarity for fixed q with normalized, sign or exact-polar LMO directions. At matched oracle budgets, five-seed CIFAR-10 HVP transport gains 1.52 accuracy points at q=1/4, but STORM loses 5.69 points. The learned diagonal predictor shows no resolved accuracy benefit. Neural experiments use Euclidean, not Muon, directions, and randomized HVP runs take 1046 versus 934 seconds despite equal oracle work. Code is promised with the final version. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Accelerated Proximal Gradient Method for Non-Strongly Convex Time-Varying Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11425) | TV-APG replaces iteration-indexed acceleration with momentum computed from current iterate displacement and uses a modified proximal step to preserve domain feasibility. For convex composite objectives on compact domains with bounded temporal variation, it improves the asymptotic tracking-gap dependence from the square-root variation rate of proximal gradient to a two-thirds power. TV-APGe additionally estimates variation through function evaluations and can perform better empirically. Tests include synthetic quartic minimization and Electricity, Traffic, and Solar autoregression, matching ten gradient queries per round. Smoothness and diameter information remain inputs, and the estimate-based guarantee requires sufficiently informative variation estimates; this is not arbitrary nonstationary neural-training theory. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Composite Online-to-Nonconvex Conversion with Optimal Oracle Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12328) | Extends online-to-nonconvex conversion to nonsmooth nonconvex objectives plus a convex regularizer, retaining the full regularizer in online losses rather than linearizing it. Composite mirror descent selects directions around fixed block anchors; stochastic interpolation queries and a final convex-model shift yield proximal Goldstein stationarity with O(delta^-1 epsilon^-3) gradient queries or O(d delta^-1 epsilon^-3) function evaluations. Ten paired trials on synthetic ReLU-regression and ramp-loss problems at dimensions 16/256/1024 show lower objectives/stationarity than Chen's minibatch/variance-reduced methods at equal query counts. Stationarity is numerically upper-bounded, frequent convex subproblems add computational overhead, and large-model/wall-clock evidence or official code is absent. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Federated Zeroth-Order Optimization with Direction Aggregation and Variance Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11821) | FedZOO aggregates zeroth-order directions evaluated along projected local trajectories, then makes one projected server update instead of averaging client models. FedVRZO retains this recursion but uses independent sample-direction pairs to improve variance scaling at a fixed local query budget. Five-seed CIFAR-10 black-box attack experiments use five fully participating clients; after 300 rounds, FedVRZO reaches attack loss 0.639 versus DZO-SVRG's 0.771. Theoretical query improvements concern the sampling component and smoothed projected stationarity, while local drift and heterogeneity remain. Growing sampling budgets and shrinking local steps enter convergence guarantees, and communication-matched attack results are not universal query- or wall-clock-matched training gains. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Matching the Lower Bounds: Stochastic Contracting Cubic Newton and Its Optimal Acceleration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11693) | Stochastic Contracting Cubic Newton solves regularized local models over a compact feasible set and contracts the next iterate toward the model solution. Its accelerated variant combines gradient-noise averaging, Hessian-error control, and higher-order acceleration to match the stochastic convex lower-bound exponents. The accelerated error separates gradient noise at inverse-square-root iteration order, Hessian noise at inverse-square order, and the exact term at inverse-three-and-a-half order. These guarantees concern convex objectives with Lipschitz Hessians and bounded-domain geometry, not arbitrary neural losses. Stochastic derivatives, calibrated regularization, and model-subproblem solutions are required; oracle optimality alone does not establish wall-clock superiority for large models. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Optimal Rates for Federated Variational Inequalities under different relaxations of bounded variance assumption](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12641) | This communication-budgeted algorithm family combines local extragradient trajectories, worker averaging, common-query minibatches, and, for composite problems, accelerated mirror-prox sliding with local monotone solves. Its contribution is the budgeted construction and interpolation-sensitive guarantees rather than a new elementary gradient kernel. Strong-growth and root-noise models yield faster stochastic terms, while composite interpolation separates accelerated gradient, local monotone, and pooled sampling contributions. The work is an oracle-complexity study rather than an LLM training benchmark. Workers share operator and sampling assumptions, problem constants enter schedules, and exact monotone evaluations can be required. Local sequential depth and total sample count must not be conflated with communication rounds. |
| 2026-10-08 | [Rounding in Preconditioner Space: Redesigning 4-bit AdamW Optimizer-State Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12444) [Code](https://github.com/nubank/adamw4bit) | Redesigns 4-bit AdamW around the update induced by stored moments: ZIP-SR rounds second moments stochastically in preconditioner space while retaining zero; ZE-EDEN instead calibrates a zero-excluding codebook. Both use NF4 first moments and stochastic LM-head rounding during the final 10% of training. | 2026-10-01 | [Trust the Direction, Search the Step: Zero-and-First-Order Methods for LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02190) [Code](https://github.com/nizswan/Zeroth-First-Order-Framework) | ZFO preserves a first-order optimizer's direction and uses two symmetric forward probes to fit a bounded one-dimensional Taylor or Pade model for step selection. Existing backward information supplies the directional derivative, avoiding Hessian-vector products but retaining first-order activation memory. In three-seed Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B experiments, the quadratic variant raises MATH accuracy from AdamW's 31.77% to 40.10%. The reported Qwen runtime overhead is about 5.2%, with higher peak memory. Higher-order models do not consistently help: cubic Taylor performs substantially worse on MATH. Guarantees require aligned directions and control of step-selection error, rather than ensuring universally better fine-tuning. |

Penalty and inner-accuracy choices yield ergodic objective and feasibility guarantees, with O(1/epsilon) gradient complexity for fixed outer iteration count under stated boundedness assumptions. The September revision proves expected network-aggregate approximate regret of order sqrt(T) up to logarithms, using T neighbor exchanges and near-linear total separation calls per agent. It improves the nonconvex rate from O(K^-1/4) to O(K^-1/3), and the star-convex rate from about O(K^-1/3) to O(K^-1/2); one 124M NanoGPT run improves loss from 3.832 to 3.682, but extra gradient-difference cost, transient spikes, and large-scale robustness remain unquantified. |

Under relaxed gradient smoothness (and Hessian smoothness for one variant) they achieve O(K^-1/3) nonconvex stationarity versus O(K^-1/4), while small MLP/LSTM experiments are mixed; no Muon-scale LLM or wall-clock study establishes whether the extra HVP pays off. |

Thick-restart Lanczos makes low-rank variants practical, but CIFAR-10 and GPT experiments mostly match rather than beat Muon, and native PyTorch support and theory explaining the empirical trade-off are absent. |

| 2025-09-19 | [Towards Simple and Provable Parameter-Free Adaptive Gradient Methods](https://openreview.net/forum?id=dHxM51W120) | AdaGrad++ and Adam++ replace a tuned global learning rate with a simple trajectory-distance scale—maximum distance from initialization, normalized by dimension and, for Adam, time—while preserving the coordinatewise preconditioning of their namesakes. In convex stochastic optimization, AdaGrad++ retains O(T^-1/2) and Adam++ matches Adam’s rate; CIFAR-10 and GPT-2 experiments show modest gains, but nonconvex guarantees are absent and the experimentally used AdamW++ has no proof. |
| 2025-09-19 | [Towards Simple and Provable Parameter-Free Adaptive Gradient Methods](https://openreview.net/forum?id=dHxM51W120) | AdaGrad++ and Adam++ replace a tuned global learning rate with a simple trajectory-distance scale—maximum distance from initialization, normalized by dimension and, for Adam, time—while preserving the coordinatewise preconditioning of their namesakes. In convex stochastic optimization, AdaGrad++ retains O(T^-1/2) and Adam++ matches Adam’s rate; CIFAR-10 and GPT-2 experiments show modest gains, but nonconvex guarantees are absent and the experimentally used AdamW++ has no proof. |
| 2025-09-15 | [Low-rank Orthogonalization for Large-scale Matrix Optimization with Applications to Foundation Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11983) [Code](https://github.com/dengzhanwang/Low-rank-Muon) | Low-rank Muon Gaussian-sketches momentum, QR-factorizes the sketch, orthogonalizes only QᵀM, then lifts the result, replacing full Newton–Schulz while suppressing noise-sensitive tail singular directions. It proves near-optimal MSGD stationarity and optimal epsilon dependence under heavy-tailed noise, while GPT-2/LLaMA tests through 1B usually beat tuned Muon at ranks 100–200; end-to-end savings are modest, small models can regress, and rank selection or safeguarded error checks add overhead. |
| 2025-09-15 | [SRON: State-free LLM Training via Row-wise Gradient Normalization](https://openreview.net/forum?id=BtQLBWr6zI) | SRON rescales each two-dimensional gradient row by its current RMS before an SGD step, targeting attention-induced row-scale disparities without momentum, SVD, or persistent state; unlike global normalization, it preserves matrix structure. Under bounded-gradient/variance assumptions it proves O(log T/√T) nonconvex stationarity, and LLaMA/GPT/Gemma tests through 7B report 90–100% less optimizer state and up to 67% less time; the best hybrid still uses Adam for nonmatrix parameters, while fully state-free SRON† loses accuracy. |
| 2025-09-03 | [Understanding and Improving Shampoo and SOAP via Kullback-Leibler Minimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03378) [Code](https://github.com/yorkerlin/KL-Methods) | KL-Shampoo jointly estimates both Kronecker covariance factors by minimizing Gaussian KL divergence, then uses infrequent QR bases and per-step EMA eigenvalue corrections, unlike Shampoo’s one-sided Frobenius-motivated factors or SOAP’s full Adam state. Across four language models up to 227M, plus a 450M check, 150-run searches show it beats Shampoo/SOAP without grafting at SOAP-like iteration cost; no optimizer convergence theorem is given, and evidence remains sub-billion-scale after unusually extensive tuning. |
| 2025-09-01 | [Learning a Zeroth-Order Optimizer for Fine-Tuning LLMs](https://openreview.net/forum?id=2Dn4yHYLQJ) | ZO Fine-tuner replaces MeZO’s fixed isotropic Gaussian sampling with tiny per-parameter-block networks that predict normalized perturbation variances from compressed model and loss statistics, meta-trained once along a first-order trajectory and then reused. A block-diagonal-Hessian argument gives a tighter one-step expected-loss bound, and four LLMs across seven datasets win 82.1% of loss comparisons at near-MeZO memory; the result is not a global convergence theorem, and diagonal variances ignore cross-parameter correlations. |
| 2025-07-15 | [AdaMuon: Adaptive Muon Optimizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.11005) [Code](https://github.com/niuxiangqi/SoftClippingMuon) | AdaMuon sign-transforms first-moment matrices before Newton–Schulz, tracks an elementwise EMA of squared orthogonal directions, divides by that RMS, and rescales to Adam-like update magnitude, adding coordinate adaptivity absent from Muon. GPT-2 and Qwen2.5 pretraining experiments through 7B report up to 40% token-efficiency gains, while an O(log T/√T) bound assumes directional alignment; the full second-moment buffer forfeits Muon’s memory advantage and adds compute. |
| 2025-06-20 | [Memory-Efficient LLM Pretraining via Minimalist Optimizer Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16659) [Code](https://github.com/OptimAI-Lab/Minimalist_LLM_Pretraining) | SCALE normalizes every matrix-gradient column and applies first-moment momentum only to the output layer, retaining essentially SGD elsewhere; this bottom-up design seeks the minimum useful state rather than compressing Adam’s two moments. LLaMA/Qwen experiments from 60M to 7B match or beat Adam, Muon, GaLore, Fira, and APOLLO using 35–45% of Adam’s total memory, with theory supporting variance-targeted momentum; evidence stops at 7B/100B tokens and the 7B comparison is a single run. |
| 2025-06-08 | [A Stable Whitening Optimizer for Efficient Neural Network Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.07254) [Code](https://github.com/kvfrans/splus) | SPlus replaces Shampoo’s inverse-square-root preconditioned gradient with a bounded instant-sign update in a historical eigenbasis, adds symmetric shape scaling for width-transferable learning rates, and averages fast iterates to suppress high-rate noise. Transformer language, vision, and diffusion tests match Adam in 44–58% of steps and 62–83% of wall time, but the empirically designed method lacks convergence theory, uses roughly 60% more memory than Adam for square layers, and still pays periodic eigendecompositions. |
| 2025-05-29 | [On the Convergence Analysis of Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.23737) | This paper does not alter Muon; it analyzes exact matrix-sign updates under Frobenius-, spectral-, and nonuniform-smoothness models, showing any advantage over GD arises when blockwise Hessians are low rank or the trajectory-averaged curvature J is small. Quadratic, MNIST, MLP, and six-layer GPT-2 experiments track the predicted ratios, but the evidence is small-scale and relies on exact orthogonalization plus structural, star-convex, or bounded-third-derivative assumptions rather than production Muon. |
| 2025-05-27 | [PolarGrad: A Class of Matrix-Gradient Optimizers from a Unifying Preconditioning Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.21799) | PolarGrad multiplies a gradient’s polar factor by its nuclear norm, restoring update magnitudes that vanish with the gradient, and replaces Muon’s tuned Newton–Schulz approximation with QDWH or ZOLO-PD. Under smooth PL objectives this yields linear convergence where constant-step matrix-sign descent reaches a floor; matrix problems and small Qwen/GPT-2 runs are favorable, but momentum/non-PL theory is left open, GPT-2 losses are often similar, and scalable distributed implementations remain future work. |
| 2025-05-22 | [The Polar Express: Optimal Matrix Sign Methods and Their Application to the Muon Algorithm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.16932) | Polar Express replaces Muon’s fixed Newton–Schulz polynomial with per-iteration odd polynomials chosen by a minimax problem over the current singular-value interval, plus safeguards for bfloat16. It proves worst-case-optimal, super-exponential polar-factor convergence and gives small but consistent GPT-2 gains over recent five-step alternatives from 1B to 10B tokens; benefits shrink with longer training, CIFAR results are inconclusive, and the method depends on a supplied lower singular-value bound. |
| 2025-05-19 | [Gluon: Making Muon & Scion Great Again! (Bridging Theory and Practice of LMO-based Optimizers for LLMs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.13416) | Gluon formalizes practical Muon/Scion as independent layer-wise linear-minimization-oracle steps with adaptive radii and introduces layer-wise (L0,L1)-smoothness, replacing prior global smoothness analyses that prescribe unusably small steps. Deterministic theory produces radii close to tuned values, stochastic convergence is also established, and 124M NanoGPT plus CIFAR-10 trajectories support the model; however, theory assumes exact LMOs and bounded variance, while adaptive-radius guarantees remain deterministic. |
| 2025-05-04 | [Practical Efficiency of Muon for Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02222) | Rather than comparing optimizers at fixed resources, this study maps compute-time Pareto frontiers across model sizes, batch sizes, and TPU counts. Muon needs about 10-15% fewer tokens than AdamW at matched training loss, with the advantage remaining beyond the critical batch size; a 3.7B model trained for 160B tokens combines Muon with maximal-update parameterization and telescoping hyperparameter transfer. The proposed tuning strategy reduces search complexity to O(C log N), but evaluation uses fresh-batch training loss as a generalization proxy, relies on an in-house JAX implementation, and provides no downstream evaluation outside language pretraining. |
| 2025-04-22 | [Muon Optimizer Accelerates Grokking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.16041) | Across small modular-arithmetic and parity tasks with softmax, stablemax, and sparsemax attention, this study reports that Muon reaches stable 95% validation accuracy earlier than AdamW: pooled mean grokking epochs are 102.89 versus 153.09, with a reported p-value of 6.33e-8. It establishes an early empirical link between orthogonalized updates and grokking but does not isolate Newton-Schulz from update scaling, pool task effects with limited per-task detail, and remains confined to small algorithmic transformers; later work provides the missing mechanism ablation. |
| 2025-04-07 | [Dion: Distributed Orthonormalized Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.05295) [Code](https://github.com/microsoft/dion) | Dion replaces Muon’s full-matrix Newton–Schulz pass with amortized power iteration on a slowly changing momentum buffer; sharding-aware low-rank factors, rank fraction, and error feedback compress both computation and communication. GPT-style models through 3B retain Muon-like convergence and hyperparameter transfer, with larger models tolerating lower rank, but extreme-scale timing is simulated rather than trained, results cover only language models, and low ranks degrade quality unless error feedback and rank are carefully chosen. |
| 2025-02-24 | [COSMOS: A Hybrid Adaptive Optimizer for Memory-Efficient Training of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17410) [Code](https://github.com/lliu606/COSMOS) | COSMOS tracks only the leading eigenspace of the gradient second moment, applies SOAP-style coordinate adaptation there, and uses a Muon matrix-sign update on the orthogonal residual, unlike either optimizer alone. LLaMA/GPT-2 tests through 1B improve perplexity with roughly 13d² state versus SOAP’s 66d² and near-Muon wall time, but there is no convergence theory, rank and mixture weights still need choices, and 1B comparisons omit SOAP because of resource limits. |
| 2025-02-24 | [Muon is Scalable for LLM Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16982) [Code](https://github.com/MoonshotAI/Moonlight) | This report makes Muon scalable by adding decoupled weight decay, shape-dependent scaling that fixes update RMS and matches AdamW’s learning-rate convention, plus a ZeRO-1-style sharded momentum implementation that gathers only the matrix being orthogonalized. Scaling-law fits estimate 52% of AdamW’s FLOPs, and Moonlight trains a 3B-active/16B-total MoE for 5.7T tokens; evidence centers on one proprietary pipeline, distributed Muon adds communication, and pretrain/finetune optimizer mismatch remains unresolved. |
| 2025-02-11 | [Training Deep Learning Models with Norm-Constrained LMOs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07529) [Code](https://github.com/LIONS-EPFL/scion) | Scion chooses architecture-aware norm balls and applies layerwise linear minimization oracles—sign for vector-like groups and spectral/polar updates for matrices—optionally constraining weights, rather than learning Adam-style geometry online. Its uSCG/SCG theory gives order-optimal O(n^-1/4) stochastic nonconvex stationarity without knowing smoothness, while NanoGPT through 3B and vision tests show learning-rate transfer, large-batch robustness, and one-weight/one-gradient memory; guarantees still assume smoothness and bounded variance, and large-scale evidence is limited to a single 3B run. |

### 2024

| Date | Paper Title | Summary |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2024-12-08 | [Muon: An optimizer for hidden layers in neural networks](https://kellerjordan.github.io/posts/muon/) [Code](https://github.com/KellerJordan/Muon) | Muon orthogonalizes Nesterov momentum for each two-dimensional weight with five GPU-friendly Newton–Schulz steps, approximating UVᵀ and therefore spectral-norm steepest descent; embeddings, heads, and vectors stay on AdamW. The blog reports a 35% NanoGPT speedrun gain and favorable 1.5B runs with sub-1% estimated FLOP overhead, but offers no convergence proof and explicitly leaves 20B-plus/trillion-token scaling, distributed execution, finetuning, and reinforcement learning untested. |
| 2024-12-02 | [PROFIT: A Specialized Optimizer for Deep Fine Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01930) | PROFIT takes temporary reference-optimizer steps from the current converged model, treats their displacement as an implicit old-task direction, projects a new-task gradient away from it when they conflict, restores the weights, then applies any main optimizer—without old data or replay. Theory gives local old-loss protection and stable-point properties, and vision/VLM/motion tests reduce forgetting, but no full convergence proof is supplied; each update needs extra batches and roughly 25% more memory in the vanilla implementation. |
| 2024-11-25 | [Cautious Optimizers: Improving Training with One Line of Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16085) [Code](https://github.com/kyleliang919/C-Optim) | Cautious optimizers mask coordinates where a momentum-based proposal disagrees in sign with the current gradient, then renormalize surviving coordinates, adding one line to AdamW, Lion, or MARS without retuning. Lyapunov/Hamiltonian analysis preserves base-optimizer descent guarantees, and LLM plus Mini-ImageNet experiments improve stability, but it does not prove a strictly faster global rate; after exhaustive tuning, 130M–1.2B perplexity gains are only 0.1–1%, and broader RL/continual-learning tests remain future work. |
| 2024-11-15 | [MARS: Unleashing the Power of Variance Reduction for Training Large Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10438) [Code](https://github.com/AGI-Arena/MARS) | MARS injects a scaled STORM correction—the same-sample gradient difference between current and previous iterates—into AdamW, Lion, or Shampoo, clips the corrected estimator, and builds preconditioners from it. Under smoothness, bounded variance, and positive-definite preconditioners it proves O(log T/T^(1/3)) convergence, while GPT-2 tests through 770M improve token and wall-clock efficiency; exact MARS needs a second gradient, so headline experiments use an approximation without the same guarantee, and evidence is confined largely to GPT-2. |
| 2024-11-11 | [Lean and Mean Adaptive Optimization via Subset-Norm and Subspace-Momentum with Convergence Guarantees](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07120) [Code](https://github.com/timmytonga/sn-sm) | Subset-Norm shares AdaGrad-style scale estimates across carefully chosen coordinate groups, reducing adaptive-state memory from O(d) to O(sqrt(d)), while Subspace-Momentum stores momentum only in a low-dimensional subspace and applies SGD in its complement. Both receive high-probability nonconvex guarantees; combined on LLaMA-1B, they reached Adam's perplexity in 6.8B rather than 13.1B tokens while cutting optimizer-state memory by over 80% with little extra tuning. |
| 2024-11-05 | [ADOPT: Modified Adam Can Converge with Any $β_2$ with the Optimal Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02853) [Code](https://github.com/iShohei220/adopt) | ADOPT breaks the harmful dependence between Adam's current gradient and current variance estimate by normalizing with the previous second moment and applying normalization before the momentum update. These small ordering changes yield the optimal O(1/sqrt(T)) nonconvex rate for any beta2 without uniformly bounded gradient noise, unlike many Adam repairs; extensive image, generative, language, and reinforcement-learning experiments also outperformed Adam-family baselines. |
| 2024-10-25 | [COAT: Compressing Optimizer states and Activation for Memory-Efficient FP8 Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19313) [Code](https://github.com/NVlabs/COAT) | COAT extends FP8 beyond linear-layer arithmetic to the two major remaining memory consumers: optimizer states and saved activations. Dynamic Range Expansion reshapes state distributions to use FP8's range accurately, while mixed-granularity activation quantization chooses per-tensor or per-group scaling by operator sensitivity; LLM/VLM training retained near-lossless quality while using 1.54x less end-to-end memory and running 1.43x faster than BF16, enabling larger batches or fewer GPUs. |
| 2024-10-21 | [LDAdam: Adaptive Optimization from Low-Dimensional Gradient Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16103) [Code](https://github.com/IST-DASLab/LDAdam) | LDAdam performs Adam updates in rotating low-rank subspaces while still exploring the full parameter space, introducing projection-aware moment transport so statistics remain meaningful when the basis changes. Block power iteration selects each subspace, and generalized error feedback corrects compression of both gradients and optimizer states; convergence follows under standard assumptions, while language-model pretraining and fine-tuning matched full Adam closely at a fraction of its state memory. |
| 2024-09-17 | [SOAP: Improving and Stabilizing Shampoo using Adam](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11321) [Code](https://github.com/nikhilvyas/SOAP) | SOAP proves that half-power Shampoo is Adafactor in Shampoo's eigenbasis, then replaces Adafactor there with Adam and continuously updates diagonal moments between infrequent basis refreshes. This prevents the degradation caused by simply making Shampoo decompositions stale and leaves only refresh frequency beyond Adam's hyperparameters; on 360M/660M language models, SOAP cut iterations by over 40% and wall time by over 35% versus AdamW, improving both metrics about 20% over Shampoo. |
| 2024-09-05 | [The AdEMAMix Optimizer: Better, Faster, Older](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03137) [Code](https://github.com/apple/ml-ademamix) | AdEMAMix challenges single-timescale momentum by mixing a fast EMA for recent gradients with a very slow EMA that preserves information for tens of thousands of steps, then schedules the older component into Adam's update. The added long memory both accelerates optimization and reduces forgetting: a 1.3B model trained on 101B tokens matched an AdamW model trained on 197B, showing that old gradients remain useful far longer than conventional momentum assumes. |
| 2024-06-24 | [Adam-mini: Use Fewer Learning Rates To Gain More](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16793) [Code](https://github.com/zyushun/Adam-mini) | Adam-mini uses observed block structure in neural-network Hessians to replace Adam's per-parameter second moments with one adaptive rate per structurally chosen block while retaining first momentum. The analysis suggests at least 99.9% of Adam's distinct rates are redundant; across 39M–13B models, pretraining, SFT, and RLHF matched or beat AdamW with roughly 50% less optimizer memory, and Llama-2-7B throughput rose 49.6% on two A800 GPUs, saving 33% wall time. |
| 2024-05-24 | [MicroAdam: Accurate Adaptive Optimization with Low Space Overhead and Provable Convergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15593) [Code](https://github.com/IST-DASLab/MicroAdam) | MicroAdam sparsifies or compresses gradients before they enter Adam's moments, then controls the lost information with error feedback whose correction buffer is itself compressed—avoiding the full-size residual that would erase the memory gain. Its analysis shifts compression effects into higher-order terms and matches AMSGrad's asymptotic guarantees for smooth nonconvex and PL objectives; GPU experiments on BERT- and LLaMA-scale models remained competitive with uncompressed Adam in convergence and runtime while using less memory. |
| 2024-05-24 | [The Road Less Scheduled](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15682) [Code](https://github.com/facebookresearch/schedule_free) | Schedule-Free optimization derives a direct equivalence between learning-rate decay and iterate averaging, then evaluates gradients at an interpolation of running and averaged iterates so a constant-rate momentum optimizer can obtain scheduled-like behavior without knowing the stopping horizon. It adds no hyperparameters to standard momentum methods, works from convex problems through large-scale deep learning, and Schedule-Free AdamW won the self-tuning track of the 2024 MLCommons AlgoPerf challenge. |
| 2024-05-21 | [FAdam: Adam is a natural gradient optimizer using diagonal empirical Fisher information](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.12807) [Code](https://github.com/lessw2020/fadam_pytorch) | FAdam derives Adam as an approximate natural-gradient method using the square root of a diagonal empirical Fisher metric, explicitly cataloguing the diagonal, empirical, and root approximations and warning that empirical Fisher is best justified for discrete log-likelihood losses. That geometry motivates corrected momentum and bias handling, adaptive epsilon, clipping, and metric-aware decay; the resulting optimizer improved LLM, VQ-VAE, and speech-recognition training, including reported state-of-the-art ASR results. |

### 2023

| Date | Paper Title | Summary |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2023-12-04 | [AGD: an Auto-switchable Optimizer using Stepwise Gradient Difference for Preconditioning Matrix](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.01658) [Code](https://github.com/intelligent-machine-learning/dlrover/tree/master/atorch/atorch/optimizers) | AGD builds its diagonal preconditioner from squared differences between consecutive gradients, treating them as inexpensive Hessian-row information, and adds a data-driven gate that switches each update between adaptive scaling and SGD-like behavior. The authors provide convergence guarantees for both regimes and show when switching occurs; experiments spanning NLP, vision, and recommendation report competitive or better predictive performance, though the evidence does not isolate a universal advantage across every architecture. |
| 2023-10-16 | [AdaLomo: Low-memory Optimization with Adaptive Learning Rate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10195) [Code](https://github.com/OpenLMLab/LOMO) | AdaLomo identifies per-parameter adaptive scaling—not momentum—as the main source of AdamW's advantage over state-free LOMO, then restores that scaling with rank-one nonnegative factorization of second moments and grouped update normalization for stability. Retaining LOMO's fused backward/update workflow keeps gradient and state memory low; instruction tuning, continued pretraining, and from-scratch pretraining matched AdamW more closely than LOMO while preserving the ability to train large models on limited hardware. |
| 2023-09-05 | [AdaPlus: Integrating Nesterov Momentum and Precise Stepsize Adjustment on AdamW Basis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.01966) [Code](https://github.com/guanleics/AdaPlus) | AdaPlus combines AdamW's decoupled decay, NAdam-style lookahead momentum, and AdaBelief's variance-of-prediction-error denominator in one update without adding hyperparameters. Across CIFAR-10 CNN classification, Penn Treebank LSTM language modeling, and CIFAR-10 GAN training it approached or slightly exceeded momentum SGD's image generalization, beat tested adaptive baselines in language modeling, and remained stable for GANs; however, the small three-task study leaves large-scale transfer unestablished. |
| 2023-07-28 | [CoRe Optimizer: An All-in-One Solution for Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.15663) [Code](https://github.com/ReiherGroup/CoRe_optimizer) | CoRe combines Adam-like moment normalization, RPROP-style sign-consistency changes to each weight's step size, and synaptic-intelligence importance scores that can freeze selected weights to balance plasticity with retention in continual learning. This paper broadens its evaluation beyond lifelong potentials, comparing nine optimizers across diverse regression/classification and scientific-learning tasks; CoRe was best or competitive throughout and required changing only one recommended hyperparameter between minibatch and full-batch regimes, though its update/state machinery is comparatively elaborate. |
| 2023-07-18 | [Promoting Exploration in Memory-Augmented Adam using Critical Momenta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.09638) [Code](https://github.com/chandar-lab/CMOptimizer) | Critical-Momenta Adam stores selected momentum vectors and reuses them to add inertia that overshoots narrow basins, replacing earlier critical-gradient buffers whose high directional variance causes cancellation near sharp minima. Toy analysis demonstrates a stronger bias toward flat regions, and experiments improve image classification, language modeling, and online continual-learning performance; the mechanism offers a cheaper exploration alternative to sharpness-aware extra gradients, at the cost of maintaining and tuning a finite memory buffer. |
| 2023-07-05 | [CAME: Confidence-guided Adaptive Memory Efficient Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.02047) [Code](https://github.com/yangluo7/CAME) | CAME starts from Adafactor's rank-one second-moment factorization but estimates confidence from the discrepancy in its raw parameter updates, using that signal to suppress factorization-induced instability while retaining near-factorized memory. It matched Adam-like convergence on GPT-2 and T5 and improved BERT pretraining at 8K and 32K batches; in large-batch BERT it reached LAMB-comparable validation accuracy with about 15% less memory, directly addressing a regime where prior memory-efficient optimizers degraded. |
| 2023-06-16 | [Full Parameter Fine-tuning for Large Language Models with Limited Resources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09782) [Code](https://github.com/OpenLMLab/LOMO) | LOMO makes full-parameter fine-tuning memory-efficient by updating each parameter as soon as its gradient is produced during backpropagation, so gradients need not coexist in memory, and by using state-free SGD with loss scaling and gradient normalization for stability. Combined with checkpointing and ZeRO-style partitioning, it reduced memory to 10.8% of a standard DeepSpeed setup and enabled full tuning of a 65B model on one eight-RTX-3090 machine, trading Adam-style adaptivity for footprint. |
| 2023-06-09 | [Prodigy: An Expeditiously Adaptive Parameter-Free Learner](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.06101) [Code](https://github.com/konstmish/prodigy) | Prodigy accelerates D-Adaptation's learning-rate-free estimation of the unknown initial distance to a solution, using a more aggressive but provably controlled lower-bound update. It improves D-Adaptation's worst-case rate by O(sqrt(log(D/d0))) and establishes a matching obstruction for slowly growing methods; across twelve logistic datasets plus vision, translation, recommendation, MRI, RoBERTa, and GPT tasks, Prodigy consistently beat D-Adaptation and approached carefully tuned Adam without a learning-rate search. |
| 2023-05-25 | [DoWG Unleashed: An Efficient Universal Parameter-Free Gradient Descent Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16284) [Code](https://github.com/AMorporkian/DoWG) | DoWG replaces AdaGrad's unweighted squared-gradient accumulator with gradients weighted by the squared distance traveled from initialization, producing a Distance-over-Weighted-Gradients step rule with no tuned scale or search/restarts. For convex objectives it automatically adapts to both smooth and nonsmooth regimes and matches optimally tuned gradient descent up to logarithmic factors; practical experiments show it self-organizes near the edge of stability, although the strongest guarantees remain convex. |
| 2023-05-25 | [Sharpness-Aware Minimization Revisited: Weighted Sharpness as a Regularization Term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.15817) [Code](https://github.com/intelligent-machine-learning/dlrover/tree/master/atorch/atorch/optimizers) | WSAM rewrites sharpness as an explicit weighted regularizer added to ordinary empirical risk, exposing a coefficient that trades training loss against neighborhood sharpness instead of treating SAM's robust objective as indivisible. A combined PAC and PAC-Bayes analysis supplies a generalization bound, while experiments across public vision and language tasks were better than or competitive with base optimizers and SAM variants; like SAM, it still requires an additional perturbation-gradient evaluation. |
| 2023-05-23 | [Sophia: A Scalable Stochastic Second-order Optimizer for Language Model Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14342) [Code](https://github.com/Liuhong99/Sophia) | Sophia divides momentum by an infrequently refreshed stochastic diagonal-Hessian estimate, then clips each preconditioned coordinate to prevent rapidly changing or negative curvature from producing destructive steps. Unlike heavyweight second-order methods, its estimator adds negligible amortized overhead; on 125M–1.5B GPT models it matched AdamW perplexity with about 50% fewer steps, compute, and wall time, while the simplified theory gives a condition-number-independent runtime bound. |
| 2023-05-09 | [UAdam: Unified Adam-Type Algorithmic Framework for Non-Convex Stochastic Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.05675) | UAdam expresses Adam, NAdam, AMSGrad, AdaBound, AdaFom, Adan, and related methods through one generalized second-moment recursion, making their convergence conditions comparable rather than analyzing variants piecemeal. For nonconvex stochastic objectives it proves convergence to a stationary-point neighborhood at an O(1/T) rate, with the neighborhood shrinking as first-moment momentum approaches one, and notably imposes no restriction on the second-moment momentum; the contribution is principally theoretical rather than a new empirical optimizer. |
| 2023-02-16 | [FOSI: Hybrid First and Second Order Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.08484) [Code](https://github.com/hsivan/fosi) | FOSI wraps an arbitrary first-order optimizer by periodically using Lanczos extreme Hessian eigenpairs to split the gradient into orthogonal subspaces: Newton steps handle selected high-curvature directions while the base optimizer handles the complement. Directly approximating the inverse preconditioner avoids inversion-amplified error, and theory shows improved conditioning; across neural-network tasks, the best FOSI variants reached matched loss in 48–77% of the base optimizer's wall time and beat K-FAC and L-BFGS. |
| 2023-02-13 | [Symbolic Discovery of Optimization Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.06675) [Code](https://github.com/google/automl/tree/master/lion) | This work casts optimizer invention as evolutionary program search, adding abstract execution, funnel selection, and simplification to bridge proxy-task and production-task generalization, and thereby discovers Lion. Lion keeps only momentum and applies a sign update, cutting state versus Adam; it saved up to 5x JFT pretraining compute and 2.3x diffusion compute, though gains were small or statistically insignificant for some language/image-text settings, strong augmentation, and small batches. |
| 2023-02-08 | [DoG is SGD's Best Friend: A Parameter-Free Dynamic Step Size Schedule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.12022) [Code](https://github.com/formll/dog) | DoG eliminates the multiplicative learning-rate parameter by setting each SGD step size to the maximum distance traveled from initialization divided by the root-sum of observed squared gradient norms, with only a small initial movement scale. A stabilized variant obtains near-optimal parameter-free stochastic-convex guarantees under locally bounded gradients; across vision and language transfer tasks, global DoG approached tuned SGD while per-layer DoG generally surpassed it and approached tuned Adam. |
| 2023-01-18 | [Learning-Rate-Free Learning by D-Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2301.07733) [Code](https://github.com/facebookresearch/dadaptation) | D-Adaptation turns the unknown distance-to-solution constant in AdaGrad-style rates into a monotonically improved empirical lower bound, automatically scaling dual-averaging, SGD, and Adam variants without line search or extra gradient/function evaluations. It is the first method in its convex Lipschitz setting to asymptotically attain the optimal O(DG/sqrt(n)) rate without multiplicative logarithmic loss, and experiments across more than a dozen vision, language, and recommendation tasks matched carefully hand-tuned learning rates. |

### 2022

| Date | Paper Title | Summary |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2022-11-17 | [VeLO: Training Versatile Learned Optimizers by Scaling Up](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.09760) [Code](https://github.com/google/learned_optimization/tree/main/learned_optimization/research/general_lopt) | VeLO scales learned optimization into a general-purpose neural optimizer, meta-training on three orders of magnitude more tasks with roughly 4,000 TPU-months and a lower-overhead hierarchical architecture that maps gradients and training context to updates without user tuning. On 83 VeLOdrome tasks it beat learning-rate-tuned Adam everywhere and exceeded 4x step speedup on about half, while broad model transfer supports versatility; the extraordinary meta-training cost remains a central practicality limitation. |
| 2022-10-21 | [Amos: An Adam-style Optimizer with Adaptive Weight Decay towards Model-Oriented Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.11693) [Code](https://github.com/google-research/jestimator) | Amos replaces AdamW's externally chosen linear schedule and constant decay with coupled adaptive learning-rate and weight-decay schedules derived from an architecture-specified target RMS scale for each parameter tensor. This model-oriented scale also factors per-variable initial learning rates and permits indefinite continuation without a preset horizon; across BERT variants and T5, Amos achieved better validation loss within at most 70% of AdamW's steps/time while using at most 51% of its slot-variable memory. |
| 2022-10-12 | [AdaNorm: Adaptive Gradient Norm Correction based Optimizer for CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.06364) [Code](https://github.com/shivram1987/AdaNorm) | AdaNorm is an optimizer-agnostic correction that tracks an exponential moving average of past gradient norms and rescales an atypically small current gradient toward that historical norm before the host optimizer updates its moments. This targets weak or unrepresentative minibatch gradients rather than changing Adam-style learning-rate adaptation; attaching it to Adam, diffGrad, RAdam, and AdaBelief improved VGG16/ResNet18/ResNet50 results on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and TinyImageNet, although evidence is confined to CNN vision benchmarks. |
| 2022-08-13 | [Adan: Adaptive Nesterov Momentum Algorithm for Faster Optimizing Deep Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.06677) [Code](https://github.com/sail-sg/Adan) | Adan reformulates Nesterov acceleration using current gradient differences, avoiding a second gradient evaluation at an extrapolated point, then builds adaptive first- and second-moment estimates from that signal with decoupled regularization. It matches the best-known O(epsilon^-3.5) stochastic-gradient complexity for nonconvex stationarity and consistently improved vision, language, and reinforcement-learning systems; on models including ViT, GPT-2, and MAE it often matched or exceeded baselines with half the epochs and tolerated batches from 1K to 32K. |
| 2022-06-14 | [Grad-GradaGrad? A Non-Monotone Adaptive Stochastic Gradient Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.06900) | GradaGrad replaces AdaGrad's always-growing squared-gradient accumulator with a stabilized term containing consecutive-gradient inner products, interpretable as a learning-rate hypergradient, so aligned gradients can increase the step while oscillating gradients decrease it. Reparameterization and clipping keep the denominator valid and yield convergence bounds comparable to AdaGrad for stochastic convex optimization; preliminary experiments demonstrate useful non-monotone recovery from undersized learning rates, but the empirical evaluation is less extensive than its theoretical development. |
