## Weakly Related / Adjacent Muon Papers

These papers mention, compare, or rely on Muon-style optimization, but their main contribution is broader than a standalone Muon-family optimizer.

- CSV: [data/muon_weakly_related.csv](./data/muon_weakly_related.csv)

| Date | Paper Title | Relation |
| :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2026-10-15 | [Does On-Policy Distillation Need to Complete Every Rollout?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21337) | STOPD randomly truncates student rollouts and uses blockwise inverse-probability weighting to preserve full-rollout gradient sums in expectation. Its Muon-specific analysis propagates gradient discrepancies through Nesterov momentum, stabilized Frobenius normalization, and finite polynomial orthogonalization using spectral divided differences, yielding a conditional one-step update-error bound. Dedicated Muon experiments with a 1.7B student and 4B teacher save 37% generated tokens and report 1.9x full-step speedup, with similar mathematical-task accuracy to complete-rollout distillation. These savings come from rollout estimation, not a faster Muon rule. Nonlinear optimizer updates are not themselves unbiased, and the bounds compare a shared previous state rather than guaranteeing identical long-run trajectories. |
| 2026-10-15 | [The Ephemeral Cost of Switching Optimizers Across LLM Training Stages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22650) | Separates optimizer mismatch from each stage's optimizer effect by varying probabilities of AdamW versus Muon updates during pretraining, continued pretraining, and supervised fine-tuning. Controlled 1.2B Llama-3 experiments use approximately 24B or 193B pretraining tokens and three SFT seeds per setting. Matching optimizers reduces forgetting, but downstream benchmark interactions are small; continued-pretraining mismatch largely disappears after learning-rate tuning. Muon is competitive on AdamW-pretrained Llama/Qwen models up to 14B, although most open-model gaps are small relative to run variability. The study concerns task-general SFT and specific training recipes, not universal task-specific fine-tuning superiority or a newly proposed optimizer. |
| 2026-10-15 | [When Does Muon Actually Win? A Paired Data-, Time-, and Tuning-Budget Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21555) | Benchmarks reference single-device Muon against AdamW under matched data, measured training time, and 23-candidate development budgets, followed by five paired seeds and frozen test evaluation. On 17.7M/33.6M language models and 2.70M vision transformers, Muon improves every equal-data validation-loss AUC and all twenty test-loss pairs. Time-normalized AUC favors Muon on vision but AdamW on language; the complete Muon call, including auxiliary AdamW, consumes 56.1-69.6% of training time. Configurations are selected for data AUC, not independently time-tuned. Single-A100 reference kernels and small models limit transfer to optimized distributed training; this is benchmark evidence, not a new optimizer. |
| 2026-10-15 | [When Is Muon Optimal? Radial Fragility and Minimax Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21549) | Tests whether a curvature-surrogate explanation of Muon's polar direction survives replacing a fixed input radius with a random radial law. Exact admissibility becomes a translated spherical-zonoid condition, with nonscalar freedom arising only from radial atoms aligned with curvature kinks. Under atomless radii, exact scaled-polar optimality requires an already scaled-isometric full-column-rank gradient. Replacing directional averaging by worst-case directional laws restores a polar-ray minimizer and reduces scale selection to a scalar convex problem. These are exact one-step surrogate results, not optimization-error estimates, practical finite-Newton-Schulz guarantees, or training-dynamics experiments; concentrated atomless inputs may still make polar updates approximately optimal. |
| 2026-10-14 | [From Spectral to Frobenius: When Muon Loses Its Spectral Implicit Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20112) | Shows that fixed finite Newton-Schulz orthogonalization with positive stabilization can change Muon's asymptotic implicit bias from spectral to Frobenius max-margin in separable linear classification, including EMA and Nesterov-style momentum. The proof represents practical updates as gradient descent plus summable perturbations. Removing stabilization alone can produce neither max-margin direction. Logarithmically increasing cubic NS iterations recovers spectral bias under the stated schedules; the original quintic requires a special randomized, gradient-dependent stabilization scheme, and fixed-stabilization quintic recovery remains open. Synthetic experiments illustrate these regimes. This is not evidence that increasing Polar Express steps improves LLM training, nor a universal adaptive-NS prescription. |
| 2026-10-14 | [Long-Horizon RL Needs Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20256) | Studies Muon within single-rollout asynchronous reinforcement learning for repository-level issue resolution, combining token-averaged policy loss, truncated importance sampling, and auxiliary length and tool-error penalties. Experiments use R2E-Gym with MiniCPM5-2B and Qwen3.5-4B, comparing Adam and Muon SPO baselines and ablations of the broader SPO++ recipe. The full configuration achieves higher late-training patch-test success in the selected runs. However, headline comparisons jointly alter several recipe components, each run collects its own trajectories, and summaries use the longest available runs or last observations when complete windows are unavailable. This work-in-progress reports training success, not a new optimizer or a general held-out SWE benchmark guarantee. |
| 2026-10-13 | [A Refresh Interval Does Not Fully Specify a Refresh Schedule: Learning-Rate-Dependent Placement in Kronecker-Preconditioned Language Model Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18695) | This study varies the placement of a fixed number of curvature refreshes in Mousse, a curvature-augmented Muon optimizer, while preserving eigendecomposition cost. Across short-horizon GPT-style models from 64M to 929M on six corpora, earlier and later placements exchange ranking as the learning rate changes; an equal-budget uniform schedule usually beats both diagnostic extremes. The reversal persists with published optimizer internals and denser refresh budgets. A spectral-width staleness score misses it, motivating full schedule reporting and learning-rate sweeps. This is an empirical schedule-comparison study, not a newly established adaptive controller or evidence that one placement universally improves long-run training. |
| 2026-10-13 | [How Inner Optimizers Shape Replica Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19064) | Analyzes how nonlinear inner optimizers affect DiLoCo replica scaling through pseudogradient covariance, transformation-before-averaging mismatch, and second-moment estimation at smaller local batches. Whitening-based SOAP and SPlus scale better than Muon, Shampoo, and AdamW, and scaling second-moment memory reduces an Adam mean-mismatch bound. On 512M LLaMA with 96 replicas and 30 local steps, validation losses are SOAP 2.676, SPlus 2.663, Muon 2.787, and AdamW 2.958; their own data-parallel baselines differ. Experiments span 178M/512M models and 8-96 replicas with optimizer-specific tuning. The results concern communication-efficient local training, not a universal ranking for conventional synchronized pretraining. |
| 2026-10-13 | [Taming Softmax at the Limits of Muon: A Unified Approach through Hilbert Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17588) | Explains why controlling a linear layer's RMS output movement, as in Muon's derivation, can inadequately control the probability distribution after softmax. Hilbert distance instead equals the variation seminorm of logit perturbations and bounds cross-entropy changes and probability redistribution. For LM heads, the induced row-diameter constraint has an expensive exact dual; a common-enclosing-ball approximation recovers RowNorm. For attention, the first-order QK constraint recovers the problem underlying Compositional Muon. Centering alone leaves standard Muon unchanged. This is a unifying theoretical framework, with no new training-speed or model-quality experiments; Hilbert constraints may overrestrict harmless relative changes to very-low-probability outcomes. |
| 2026-10-13 | [Which Optimizer Leaks Most? Optimizer Implicit Bias as a Training-Data Privacy Controller](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18294) | Connects checkpoint training-data reconstruction to optimizer-dependent KKT conditions and the continuous weight information retained by their implicit biases. Spectral Muon and Euclidean SGD conditions support reconstruction, whereas idealized Adam's infinity-norm bias can make the evaluated stationarity system locally underdetermined. On 500-example binary CIFAR-10, no-prior SSIM@30 is 0.5508 for Muon, 0.2287 for SGD, and 0.1379 for Adam. Experiments use small fully connected MNIST/CIFAR models; Muon only updates hidden matrices, and training-budget/architecture effects alter its advantage. Adam's resistance applies to this attack family and idealized regime, not differential privacy or a guarantee against other checkpoint attacks. |
| 2026-10-12 | [From Parallel to Sequential: Small-Batch Noise Restores Simplicity Bias of Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16868) | Shows that stochastic noise can qualitatively change spectral descent: full-batch Muon learns singular modes in parallel, while noise smooths the sign operator and restores gradient-dependent, sequential feature learning, especially at greater depth. Deep-linear expected-flow analysis derives depth-dependent implicit regularization under structured noise assumptions; symmetric heavy-tailed noise can retain the smoothing mechanism. Matrix reconstruction, DeiT, and 162M LLaMA experiments associate smaller batches with lower effective rank. At fixed learning rate, LLaMA token accuracy falls from 40.79% at batch 256 to 40.18% at 1024, but linear learning-rate scaling restores 40.78%. Thus rank trends do not imply that smaller batches universally improve quality. |
| 2026-10-10 | [Non-Euclidean Gradient Descent Has Many Edges of Stability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15223) | This paper analyzes fixed-step non-Euclidean descent on quadratic objectives through radial and projective dynamics. Unlike Hilbertian geometry's sharp almost-everywhere stability threshold, different attracting directions can produce coexisting convergent and divergent basins. A Muon-motivated spectral-norm appendix proves a local collapse of direction-selection and radial-stability thresholds at full-rank critical matrices. Crucially, the authors distinguish their duality-map iteration from Muon's normalized polar update, so these results are not a direct stability theorem for practical momentum Muon or finite Newton–Schulz implementations. Smoothness, nondegeneracy, full-rank locality, and simplified quadratic dynamics limit transfer; examples illustrate geometry rather than establish an LLM learning-rate prescription. |
| 2026-10-09 | [On the Depth Transfer of Neural Optimizers via L-Smoothness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14344) | Derives depth-uniform Lipschitz and smoothness bounds for residual networks with inverse-depth branch scaling, fixed width, bounded parameter domains, and uniform branch regularity, using arbitrary operator-norm geometries relevant to Muon, MOGA, and AdamW. These bounds remove an additional depth-dependent curvature penalty but do not alone guarantee an identical optimal learning rate. GPT-2 sweeps at width 256 cover 2-128 layers and 315M tokens per run; Muon/MOGA share nearly the same best tested rates. CIFAR-100 ResNets provide additional support. Validation-loss transfer is less consistent than training-loss transfer, and larger-model or joint width-depth guarantees are not established by these experiments. |
| 2026-10-08 | [From Geometry to Generalization: Why Row Normalization Can Beat Adam and Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11309) | Connects optimizer-selected max-margin geometry to population accuracy in high-dimensional multiclass Gaussian classification. Row-normalized updates better preserve class-mean decision directions than exact-SVD Muon or coordinate-wise Adam under stated isotropic and independently oriented anisotropic models; coordinate-aligned covariances can reverse the Adam comparison. In five-seed frozen-backbone LLaMA-60M head retraining, RowSGDM reaches perplexity 28.6466 versus Muon's 28.7027 and Adam's 28.7199, with a similar 130M ordering. This analyzes existing update families rather than introducing a new optimizer. Guarantees use stylized linear readouts, directional convergence assumptions and zero-stabilizer Adam; random-reshuffling Adam is handled through a proxy, not a general practical-Adam theorem. |
| 2026-10-06 | [The Best Optimizer Depends on Batch Size](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08975) | Tests hundreds of Muon hyperparameter-scaling rules and independently retunes optimizers across batch sizes. No single rule transfers across language modeling and CIFAR-5M; NanoGPT's roughly 1.7B-token benchmark changes winner from SOAP at 128K-token batches to Shampoo at 2M. A noisy-quadratic analysis links this reversal to curvature/noise-dependent preconditioning bias-variance tradeoffs. In checkpoint branching, keeping small-batch updates in the sharpest 0.001% of directions removes 35-59.5% of the local large-batch loss penalty. This costly fixed-subspace diagnostic is not a ready-to-use optimizer, does not establish universal rankings, and omits noise anisotropy when selecting directions. No official implementation was linked. |
| 2026-10-05 | [On the Comparison of Optimizers for Imbalanced Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06421) | This study compares continuous-time Euclidean, sign, spectral, and Newton geometries when minority structure is hidden inside the aggregate loss. Explicit minority-awareness regions and entry-time bounds show milder minority-amplitude dependence for normalized or spectral descent, without proving universal earlier entry on each instance. Practical tests include Muon and AdamW against SGD on CIFAR-10 and SmallGPT2/WikiText-103. Reported CIFAR minority accuracies average early iterations, not final accuracy, and classification uses zero momentum across three seeds. The theory idealizes small-step dynamics rather than finite Newton-Schulz Muon; language-model rare-token improvements can trade against majority loss. It supplies optimizer-geometry evidence, not a new optimizer. |
| 2026-10-03 | [Optimizer Geometry Sets the Pace: Spectral Learning Dynamics in Matrix Factorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04249) | Derives singular-mode birth, saturation and decay laws in deep matrix factorization under explicit alignment/balance conditions. Ideal momentum-free Muon removes the divergent small-initialization birth barrier and eliminates unsupported aligned modes in finite time, unlike GD's slow tails. Cumulative Shampoo retains an initial depth barrier before history-driven catch-up; K-FAC damping restores GD-like behavior below a spectral threshold. Deterministic double-precision deep-linear experiments validate the regimes with separately tuned integration steps and matched final losses. It proposes no new optimizer or practical performance ranking, and exact polar Muon, instantaneous Adam, cumulative Shampoo and a specified K-FAC block model differ from standard stochastic implementations. |
| 2026-10-02 | [Muon Learns Facts Better: Understanding the Role of Spectral Orthogonalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02798) | Analyzes population gradient flow in a single-head linear attention factual-recall model with fixed orthonormal embeddings. Spectral gradient flow, an idealized Muon limit, reduces the subject-versus-relation learning-time ratio from approximately sqrt(S/R) to order one and accelerates component error decay. Both ordinary and spectral flows respect orthogonal embedding transformations, whereas the Sign-flow approximation to Adam can change or reverse feature-learning order. ODE simulations vary subject/relation counts from four to 1,024 and corroborate these laws. This is not evidence that production Muon always learns facts better: minibatch noise, finite Newton-Schulz, realistic embeddings, deep transformers and ordinary Adam momentum are outside the analyzed setting. |
| 2026-10-02 | [OptiSelect: How does the Optimizer Shape Data Curriculum?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03432) | OptiSelect analyzes how optimizer-induced candidate utility scores shape online data selection. Under Gaussian scoring assumptions, it relates attainable selection gain to score discriminability and studies sign collapse and radial invariance of the ideal polar derivative. Experiments on 124M and 720M language models find AdamW's diagonal-adaptive scoring strongest even when Muon performs optimization. Muon's selection benefit varies with scale rather than disappearing universally. The theoretical polar-tangent map is not the implemented right-preconditioned proxy, which retains radial components, nor the exact finite Newton-Schulz update. Main gains concern applied-token efficiency, not compute savings: oversampled candidates and scoring work must also be counted. |
| 2026-10-02 | [Training-Loss Guarantees for Muon with Finite-Step Newton-Schulz Orthogonalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03306) | This analysis proves training-loss hitting guarantees for momentum Muon with five tuned Newton–Schulz iterations in sufficiently wide two-layer ReLU networks with fixed random output weights. It transfers update alignment near initialization to current-gradient descent using kernel stability, without exact polar orthogonalization. The target-dependent learning rate scales with one minus momentum, and the iteration bound worsens as momentum approaches one; it does not prove acceleration. Thirty teacher-student runs across six widths support alignment and stability diagnostics. The sufficient theoretical width is astronomically larger than tested widths, and full-batch lazy-training assumptions do not establish convergence or benefits for practical LLM pretraining. |
| 2026-10-01 | [Muon meets Tamed Langevin: Momentum Preconditioning beyond Convex and gradient-Lipschitz Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02158) | Builds a kinetic Langevin sampler from a nonquadratic spectral momentum energy, adding state-dependent noise and a divergence correction so the continuous process preserves the target Gibbs position marginal; its zero-temperature limit recovers an epsilon-regularized Muon-type update. It proves exponential continuous-time convergence in weighted total variation and uniform-in-time discrete momentum bounds for smooth semiconvex potentials with polynomial gradient/Hessian growth, convexity at infinity, and sufficiently small steps. This is substantive Muon-adjacent sampling theory rather than an empirically validated training optimizer: there are no benchmark experiments or linked code, and discrete moment stability alone does not imply an exact invariant distribution or global optimization convergence. |
| 2026-09-30 | [Convergence of Practical Muon with Finite Newton-Schulz Iterations and Nesterov Momentum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39595) | This analysis treats layer-wise finite-step Newton–Schulz Muon and Nesterov momentum jointly on a coupled nonconvex objective. Under gradient-dependent generalized smoothness and conditionally unbiased stochastic gradients with bounded layer-wise variance, it bounds the expected average Frobenius gradient norm by O(T^-1/4). Unlike exact-polar idealizations, the proof requires neither bounded stochastic gradients, symmetric noise, nor a positive lower bound on nonzero output singular values. Scalar-map bounds are verified for the practical five-step quintic and allow varying coefficients satisfying the same conditions. Vanishing stationarity uses coupled learning-rate and momentum schedules; the theorem is not a guarantee for arbitrary constant hyperparameters or an empirical optimizer superiority claim. |
| 2026-09-30 | [The Conflict Between Logic and Memory: Training Conditions for Optimizer-Dependent Rule Acquisition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00403) | This study compares optimizer-dependent acquisition of generalizing relations in shallow ReLU networks using nuisance-heavy composite tasks, parameter freezing, and a three-seed parity scan. Fixing nuisance-connected first-layer weights raises the hardest-stage accuracy from near chance to 70.56%, 68.47%, and 69.00% for momentum SGD, Adam, and Muon. On another task, the same intervention reduces Muon's rare-mode advantage over momentum SGD by 12.48 points. Post-training masking does not reproduce the benefit, distinguishing learning conditions from inference expression. Condition-specific validation selects learning rates and checkpoints, so results diagnose mechanisms rather than establish a universally superior optimizer or a new optimization rule. |
| 2026-09-30 | [The Row Normalization Puzzle in Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39114) | This analysis isolates how NorMuon's row normalization changes gradient alignment in operator-norm geometry. A rank-one construction yields a row-dimension-dependent deterministic lower bound, matched by an upper bound; the penalty persists with exact polar factors and fixed momentum, and approximate-polar stochastic bounds are also derived. Synthetic tests reproduce the slowdown, while pretraining at 286M, 1.38B, and 6.44B total parameters favors NorMuon in validation loss. The 1.38B comparison includes three seeds; CIFAR-10 separates accuracy from loss gains. The result is a worst-case/practice tension, not a new optimizer or evidence that normalization universally hurts training. |
| 2026-09-29 | [Making Analog Training Scale: Co-Designing Mapping, Optimizer, and Converters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36584) | An analog-training simulation study co-designing logical weight coordinates, optimizer-to-pulse conversion, residual accumulation, mapping, and converters rather than introducing a new standalone optimizer. It evaluates existing first-order and matrix optimizers, including Muon and Moonlight, and reports a 123.6M-parameter scaling exponent of -0.231 versus digital training's -0.238. A persistent loss gap remains, hardware results are simulated, and optimizer-specific pulse conversion complicates comparison. Relevant to deployment of matrix optimizers on analog hardware, but not evidence of a generally superior digital update rule. No paper-specific official implementation was located. |
| 2026-09-29 | [Optimizer-dependent training dynamics converge to the same one-third optimal data scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37745) | Studies online single-layer teacher–student softmax learning: optimizer-dependent radial and tangential exponents satisfy 2αr+αt=1, producing a common optimally tuned one-third data-scaling envelope across seven optimizers. Muon's fitted envelope exponent is 0.332±0.003, but its effective dynamics are inferred empirically rather than derived from the update rule; the result is a toy-model scaling analysis, not a deep-LLM optimizer or training recipe. |
| 2026-09-29 | [Second-Moment Stochastic Approximation Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36600) | Unifies Muon, ASGO, and Adam as approximations to optimal second-moment preconditioning under different gradient-noise structures, then separates nominal dynamics from estimator bias and variance. Under an aiming condition and bounded deviation it proves almost-sure convergence to a neighborhood, not generally to the exact solution. A 1.81B LLaMA-style model trained on 32 H100s illustrates alignment and hyperparameter effects, but the target is approximated by the final checkpoint; this analyzes existing methods rather than introducing a distinct optimizer. |
| 2026-09-29 | [Why Adaptive Optimizers Underestimate Rare Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37535) | Analyzes softmax output-layer fixed points and mean-embedding conservation: Muon and Shampoo preserve the stated conservation property, while several adaptive/sign methods do not. Periodic-unigram RMSProp can systematically underestimate rare tokens, and increasing Adam's second-moment memory mitigates bias in small synthetic language models. Evidence is unigram and Markov-chain data with a width-64 residual MLP rather than real LLM pretraining, and no new optimizer or public repository is established. |
| 2026-09-28 | [Can Representation Learning Decouple from Loss Minimization? Polar Updates Have an Answer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.36240) | Shows that full-batch exact-polar updates can keep improving teacher-subspace alignment during long period-two, flat-loss trajectories; projected/refitted loss can be 5–11 times below the apparent plateau. Proofs cover constrained linear and mean-field ReLU settings, with shallow-network and residual controls. Momentum, minibatch noise, and approximate Newton–Schulz transforms alter or disrupt the studied dynamics, so this is not evidence that practical Muon should deliberately tolerate a prolonged pretraining-loss plateau. |
| 2026-09-28 | [Muon Sublates the Edge of Stability in LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34915) | Derives a loss-neutral stochastic polar-update boundary 2ρ_b/η and distinguishes within-step conditional stability from temporal update alignment. In 130M and 1B language-model pretraining, Muon can track this edge without deterministic period-two reversal; larger batches strengthen only partial cancellation. The theory omits momentum and practical NS/auxiliary-optimizer details create numerical gaps. Official training and conditional-loss/alignment diagnostic code is public, with expensive reference-loss and exact-SVD probes. |
| 2026-09-27 | [Convergence of Practical Muon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33152) | Analyzes practical quintic Newton–Schulz Muon with decoupled weight decay as right-preconditioned descent with a dynamic weighted-L2 term, obtaining an expected Frobenius-gradient O(T^-1/4) nonconvex rate. Its stated dimension dependence improves on a compared AdamW bound, but norm choices, smoothness/variance assumptions, and horizon-tuned hyperparameters constrain the comparison. ResNet-32 and 124M GPT-2 experiments illustrate theory rather than establishing a new optimizer or a universal NS-iteration prescription. |
| 2026-09-26 | [Muon Under Gradient Noise and the Limits of Orthogonalization Near Optima](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32861) | Near a quadratic optimum under Gaussian gradient noise, the mean polar response becomes locally linear while a residual adds stationary covariance; momentum reduces but does not remove it. Frozen Tiny Shakespeare gradient-pool diagnostics find covariance ratios around 1.42–1.45 without momentum and 1.13–1.14 with Nesterov 0.95, with similar exact-polar and NS-5 results. No optimizer-switch training experiment, practical switch detector, or learning-rate-matching rule is provided, so this is local noise analysis rather than a validated late-training replacement. |
| 2026-09-23 | [NS-ATTENTION: Newton-Schulz Transformations of Attention Outputs in Vision Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27735) | Applies one Muon-inspired Newton–Schulz polynomial step to each attention head's feature-by-token output while preserving its Frobenius norm; this is an activation transform rather than an optimizer. Across ViT/Swin on CIFAR-10/100 it gains 0.25–0.83 points and wins all 12 paired seeds, but forward latency rises 41–104% and evaluation uses only three seeds and two small datasets. The September-25 v3 now links the official NS-Attention implementation, configurations, sanity tests, and raw classification/spectral/timing results, correcting the initial release's lack of code. |
| 2026-09-21 | [Muon Can Outperform Dedicated Continual Learning Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24678) | Applies unchanged Muon to incremental LoRA rather than proposing an optimizer: IncLoRA overall accuracy rises from 75.83±4.33 with AdamW to 81.24±2.80, and TRACE gains 3.42–7.62 points across methods. The study attributes the result to broader update spectra, but ranking among dedicated continual-learning methods remains unresolved, TRACE uses only three seeds and one order, penalty strengths are not retuned, and no official code is linked. |
| 2026-09-19 | [Optimizers for Diffusion Models: A Controlled Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23055) | Benchmarks seven existing optimizers across four diffusion formulations with matched tuning and three-seed retraining, rather than proposing an optimizer. Muon reaches LM1B perplexity 61.6 versus AdamW's 68.2 and QM9 2.069 versus 2.072, but does not beat tuned AdamW on text8 or CelebA-64. Restricted signed-permutation choices recover Shampoo/Muon updates, and MNIST autoencoder plus 10M nanoGPT curves nearly overlap Muon while beating Adam, making Muon a special case and validation target; the Taylor approximation, Hessian-eigenvector connection, and behavior beyond small models lack formal guarantees. |

Muon is a second core mechanism: under full-row-rank conditions its orthogonalized router update gives expert rows equal norms and decorrelated directions, and toy MoE pretraining preserves balance better than AdamW; it does not enforce balanced assignments, and evidence stops well short of production-scale MoE training. |
| 2026-05-29 | [Mellum2 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31268) | Mellum 2 is an open 12B/2.5B-active coding MoE combining sparse experts, mostly sliding-window attention, an MTP draft head, a 10.65T-token curriculum, 128K extension, and SFT/RL variants. Muon is a production pretraining component rather than the model’s novelty: 105B-token controls show Moonlight-scaled distributed Muon improves dense and MoE validation loss by about 2.5% versus AdamW, while default scaling diverges on dense 7B, so stability depends materially on the Muon scale configuration. |
| 2026-05-29 | [Momentum Acceleration of Normalized Steepest Descent at the Edge of Stability](https://openreview.net/forum?id=SLrFbLmJXe) | For normalized steepest descent at the Edge of Stability, momentum filters the oscillating sharp-direction component so a stable progress direction dominates the unit-norm update; a two-dimensional quadratic proves the mechanism, and momentum becomes essential for the sign-based variant. Muon is a motivating spectral member of the NSD-with-momentum family and receives an appendix ablation on the same full-batch teacher–student MLP, where momentum lowers loss and delays instability; no Muon-specific theorem, stochastic study, or large-model validation is provided. |
| 2026-05-29 | [On the Mean-field Analysis of Normalized Steepest Descent via Linear Minimization Oracles](https://openreview.net/forum?id=zs7KEqblSv) | The paper formulates normalized steepest descent for two-layer mean-field neural networks as a linear-minimization-oracle flow in Wasserstein space, proving global convergence and finite-time arrival for normalized dynamics, then non-asymptotic guarantees for finite-particle, discrete, Langevin, and adaptive-LMO schemes. Muon is a motivating spectral-norm instance of the general geometry rather than an evaluated algorithm; the results concern idealized mean-field models and exact oracles, without momentum, Newton–Schulz approximation, or large-network experiments. |
| 2026-05-29 | [Rank-One Potential Geometry for Normalized Optimizers](https://openreview.net/forum?id=6SItNX4vnH) | A rank-one orthonormal decomposition of momentum with an ℓ1-type coefficient potential places SGD, Lion, and Muon in one Lion-K/Lyapunov geometry, then extends the analysis to explicitly time-dependent potentials. Muon is a covered special case and auxiliary comparison, while the proposed ROBN resamples its orthogonal basis over time; ViT-B/16 on ImageNet-1K trains stably and beats tuned AdamW, but the empirical claim rests on one architecture and dataset rather than broad optimizer comparisons. |
| 2026-05-29 | [Understanding Polyak's Momentum in Deep Learning May Require Rethinking Non-Convex Optimization](https://openreview.net/forum?id=by4RD1Gkrn) | Worst-case lower bounds show that standard averaged or best-iterate gradient-norm metrics can rank heavy-ball methods worse than their non-momentum counterparts, with the separation worsening as β approaches one despite momentum’s practical benefit. Muon is a direct theoretical case: diagonal matrix instances reduce it to Signum, transferring the lower bound in nuclear-gradient norm; CIFAR-10 illustrates the theory–practice mismatch, but the constructions are deliberately worst-case and provide no positive explanation for deep-learning acceleration. |
| 2026-05-28 | [On the Optimizer Dependence of Neural Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29387) | In random-feature regression, optimizer preconditioning changes the fitted model-size loss exponent, not merely a constant: at language-like spectral decay, full natural gradient gives α≈0.31 versus GD’s 0.12, and a matrix-sign proxy shows similar trends across 2,400 controlled runs. Muon is represented only by the idealized preconditioner (FᵀF)⁻¹ᐟ² and contextualized against published results; the mechanism is an informal spectral heuristic, with no feature learning, actual Newton–Schulz updates, wall-clock accounting, or LLM-scale validation. |
| 2026-05-27 | [A hybrid transformer–zero-shot learning framework with Muon optimization for intelligent channel estimation in MIMO wireless systems](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-025-33791-0) | The paper combines a Transformer encoder, semantic zero-shot projection, and a training rule it calls Muon for MIMO channel estimation, reporting 22–30% lower MSE than Transformer-ZSL at middle and high SNR and robustness to unseen fading and pilot sparsity. Muon is therefore a claimed core component, not merely a comparator; however, its description emphasizes dynamic learning rates, curvature, and adaptive regularization rather than standard momentum orthogonalization, making implementation and attribution unclear alongside higher compute and simulation-only evaluation. |
| 2026-05-27 | [Parallax: Parameterized Local Linear Attention for Language Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29157) | Parallax replaces Local Linear Attention’s per-token regression solve with a learned query-like probe of KV covariance and supplies a streaming decode kernel that matches or beats FlashAttention 2/3; 0.6B and 1.7B pretraining shows parameter- and compute-matched perplexity gains. Muon is an enabling optimizer rather than a baseline footnote: it opens the correction gate and preserves probe-circuit rank, whereas Parallax is roughly tied with softmax attention under AdamW; the mechanism remains unexplained and scaling stops at 1.7B. |
| 2026-05-26 | [Extra-Merge: Tracing the Rank-1 Subspace of Model Merging in Language Model Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26484) | Extra-Merge observes that late-stage averaged checkpoints, unlike noisy raw iterates, lie on a nearly one-dimensional manifold explaining over 94% of variance; it estimates that tangent and extrapolates without gradients, with a river-valley analysis treating averaging as a high-curvature low-pass filter. GPT-2/LLaMA tests through 1.55B and Pythia-12B downstream tasks beat averaging baselines, while Muon serves only as an optimizer-agnostic transfer check; guarantees depend on the local landscape model and saved late-stage checkpoints. |
| 2026-05-26 | [How the Optimizer Shapes Learned Solutions in Equivariant Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27662) | Across EGNN, PointNet, and DGCNN on ModelNet40 and its corruption suite, replacing Adam with Muon improves clean accuracy by 1.96–5.17 points and corrupted accuracy by 2.58–4.36 points; Muon checkpoints also show higher stable/effective representation rank and larger Hessian summaries but visually more regular local basins. Here Muon is the main experimental intervention, not a derived method, and molecular results are less consistent; the four-seed, small-scale study is descriptive rather than causal or theoretical. |
| 2026-05-26 | [The Stability of Singular Distribution: A Spectral Perspective on the Two-Phase Dynamics of Language Model Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26489) | Stability of Singular Distribution tracks trace-normalized weight spectra and finds they stabilize well before weights, synchronizing with the transition from rapid to slow language-model loss descent. A simplified-Transformer analysis bounds post-threshold improvement by spectral-distribution variation, while GPT-2/LLaMA studies across schedules, decay, AdamW, and Muon support the pattern; Muon is a robustness case and spectral-scale interpretation, not the proposed method, and the theorem relies on nondegeneracy, bounded-gradient, smoothness, and margin assumptions. |
| 2026-05-24 | [YOLO26 vs. YOLOv8: A Comprehensive Architectural Benchmark of Next-Generation Real-Time Object Detection Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24831) | This independent five-scale benchmark finds YOLO26’s NMS-free, DFL-free design more accurate and compact than YOLOv8 on Pascal VOC, but only marginally better on small-object-heavy VisDrone and consistently slower on the tested GPU, with YOLO26-s at 8.38 ms versus 6.92 ms. Muon is an inherited YOLO26 component through MuSGD/CSP-Muon, not separately controlled, so results cannot isolate optimizer effects; dataset conversion, one hardware setup, and severe small-object failure constrain the deployment claims. |
| 2026-05-23 | [Momentum Streams for Optimizer-Inspired Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.24425) | Treating depth as optimization, this work replaces the Transformer residual recurrence with optimizer templates; triple-momentum TMMFormer beats vanilla, Nesterov, Adam-, Muon-, and SOAP-inspired variants on TinyStories and OpenWebText, with ablations and a local Jacobian model attributing gains to momentum rather than preconditioning. Muon appears both as the default matrix-parameter trainer and as MuonFormer’s architectural template, but optimizer swaps preserve TMMFormer’s advantage and MuonFormer adds runtime without gains; evidence is limited to small pretraining and local theory. |
| 2026-05-20 | [Same Architecture, Different Capacity: Optimizer-Induced Spectral Scaling Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21803) | Holding GPT-2 architecture and data fixed, the study finds optimizer-dependent FFN spectral-capacity laws: at 160M parameters, hard-rank scaling rises from β=0.29 under AdamW to 0.82 under Muon, while NorMuon and full-rank Dion are similar; learning-rate and extended-training controls do not erase the gap, and 350M replicates it. Muon is a principal comparison revealing representation geometry, not a new component; conclusions rest on two small scales, fitted spectral proxies, and no causal link to downstream capability. |
| 2026-05-18 | [Scale-Invariant Neural Network Optimization: Norm Geometry and Heavy-Tailed Noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18528) | In spectral-norm geometry with p-th-moment heavy-tailed noise, the paper proves a dimension-dependent lower bound and a matching batched-Scion rate O(min{m,n}ε⁻⁽³ᵖ⁻²⁾ᐟ⁽ᵖ⁻¹⁾); gradient transport under Hessian Lipschitzness improves the ε exponent. Muon is a spectral-LMO, scale-invariant special case and experimental baseline, while transported NorMuon modestly improves nanochat loss through 1.39B and some CNN metrics; the theory studies idealized polar updates, transport needs stronger smoothness, and the largest language model has one seed. |
| 2026-05-09 | [Navigating LLM Valley: From AdamW to Memory-Efficient and Matrix-Based Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09176) | This survey organizes LLM optimizers into adaptive, memory-efficient, curvature-aware, sign-based, low-rank, and matrix-based families, emphasizing that no method is universally best. Muon is only a matrix-based case study, not a new contribution; the paper argues that optimizer claims require scale-aware comparisons of validation loss, token and wall-clock efficiency, memory, stability, tuning fairness, and implementation complexity. |
| 2026-05-08 | [Flatness and Gradient Alignment Are Both Necessary: Spectral-Aware Gradient-Aligned Exploration for Multi-Distribution Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07914) | SAGE derives an excess-risk decomposition for multi-distribution learning with independent curvature and gradient-alignment terms, then targets them using layerwise polar-factor perturbations and disagreement-scaled descent noise. It reaches 78.9% average accuracy on DomainBed and generally improves base multi-task solvers, while Muon is merely a neighboring polar-orthogonalization baseline; SAGE adds per-distribution gradients and Newton-Schulz overhead, and its theory-to-mechanism link remains motivational. |
| 2026-05-07 | [Optimizer-Model Consistency: Full Finetuning with the Same Optimizer as Pretraining Forgets Less](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06654) | The paper identifies optimizer-model consistency: full fine-tuning with the same optimizer family used in pretraining gives a better learning-versus-forgetting Pareto frontier than mismatched optimizers or LoRA. Muon is a case study rather than a proposed method: despite strong GPT-2 pretraining, its rote-memorization bias hurts low-data reasoning fine-tuning; most controlled evidence is small-scale, with only AdamW consistency tested on Llama-2-7B. |
| 2026-04-26 | [DeepSeek-V4: Towards Highly Efficient Million-Token Context Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19348) | DeepSeek-V4 is a model-and-systems report introducing 1.6T/49B-active and 284B/13B-active MoE models with hybrid compressed attention, manifold-constrained hyper-connections, and native million-token context; at that length, V4-Pro uses 27% of DeepSeek-V3.2's single-token FLOPs and 10% of its KV cache. Muon is an existing training component, applied to most modules with a hybrid Newton-Schulz implementation, not the report's core algorithmic novelty. |
| 2026-04-16 | [Benchmarking Optimizers for MLPs in Tabular Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.15297) | This controlled benchmark tunes 15 optimizers on 17 tabular datasets across five MLP families, then evaluates selected configurations over ten seeds. Muon, a tested optimizer rather than a new algorithm, improves over same-architecture AdamW with a 55/25/5 win/tie/loss record, but incurs 1.18–2.98× tuning-time overhead depending on architecture; EMA is a cheaper, less consistent alternative, and results do not cover non-MLP tabular models. |
| 2026-04-14 | [Nucleus-Image: Sparse MoE for Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.12163) | Nucleus-Image introduces a 17B sparse-MoE diffusion transformer that activates about 2B parameters, uses Expert-Choice routing, decouples timestep-aware assignment from timestep-conditioned computation, and shares text KV across denoising steps. Trained on 1.5B pairs from 700M images, it matches or exceeds leading models on three benchmarks without preference post-training; Muon and its diffusion-specific parameter grouping are training-recipe components, not a new optimizer contribution. |
| 2026-04-02 | [Does Your Optimizer Care How You Normalize? Normalization-Optimizer Coupling in LLM Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01563) | A controlled 3×2 study at 1B parameters shows a silent normalization-optimizer interaction: Derf's gap to RMSNorm grows from 0.31 nats with AdamW to 0.97 with Muon, whereas Dynamic Tanh avoids the penalty. Muon is the experimental optimizer factor, not a new method; reduced Derf alpha and an EMA scale blend recover about 80% and 84% of the gap, but evidence covers only 1,000 steps and roughly 1B tokens. |
| 2026-03-30 | [Spectral Edge Dynamics: An Analytical-Empirical Study of Phase Transitions in Neural Network Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28964) | This work models phase transitions through spectral gaps in rolling-window parameter-update Gram matrices, deriving gap dynamics, a spectral loss decomposition, and an adiabatic stability parameter. Gap changes precede 24/24 grokking runs with weight decay versus 1/24 controls, while active rank stays at most three; Muon is only an optimizer-dependent example, producing k*=1 versus AdamW's k*=2 on the same 51M-parameter TinyStories model. |
| 2026-02-25 | [veScale-FSDP: Flexible and High-Performance FSDP at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22437) | veScale-FSDP is a distributed-training systems contribution combining arbitrary-granularity RaggedShard tensors, a structure-aware sharding planner, and a global distributed buffer for zero-copy communication. Across dense and sparse LLM workloads it reports 5–66% higher throughput and 16–30% lower memory than existing FSDP systems; Muon is a supported workload and convergence case study whose full matrices are gathered to balanced root ranks, not a newly proposed optimizer. |
| 2026-02-07 | [Towards Robust Scaling Laws for Optimizers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07712) | The paper shows that independently fitting Chinchilla laws per optimizer is ill-conditioned, then shares power-law exponents while learning optimizer-specific model- and data-efficiency rescalings. This parameterization reduces fit variance and extrapolation error across two architectures, two datasets, and five optimizers, while a convex-quadratic analysis derives the same loss decomposition; Muon is one comparison point alongside AdamW, Shampoo, Scion, and SOAP, not a new method. |
| 2026-01-31 | [Data Distribution as a Lever for Guiding Optimizers Toward Superior Generalization in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00576) | Theory on multi-head linear self-attention links SAM's improved generalization to lower simplicity bias, motivating a cheap data-side surrogate: use a small proxy model's loss trajectories to identify and upsample or augment late-learned examples. Across four LLMs on mathematical reasoning, targeted upsampling yields relative gains up to 18%; AdamW and Muon are downstream optimizers demonstrating broad compatibility, not contributions of the method itself. |
| 2026-01-14 | [Advancing Model Refinement: Muon-Optimized Distillation and Quantization for LLM Deployment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09865) | This edge-deployment pipeline combines teacher-generated data, KL-based distillation, LoRA, Bayesian tuning, and GPTQ W4A16 quantization, reducing a Llama-3.2-3B student from 6GB to 3GB and lowering per-token latency by up to 50%. Muon is one fine-tuning ingredient: its models lose less accuracy than Adam variants after quantization on six of eight benchmarks, but the multi-stage design does not isolate a new Muon algorithm or establish causality for that robustness. |
| 2026-01-08 | [Learnable Multipliers: Freeing the Scale of Language Model Matrix Layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.04890) | Learnable scalar, row, and column multipliers free matrix-layer scales from the gradient-noise/weight-decay equilibrium and generalize fixed muP multipliers without inference overhead. On 200GT Falcon-H1-0.5B runs they raise average downstream scores by 1.21 points with Adam and 1.10 with Muon, with larger gains on reasoning tasks; Muon is a base optimizer used to show the reparameterization transfers, not the paper's proposed optimization rule. |
| 2025-12-16 | [Optimizing Rank for High-Fidelity Implicit Neural Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.14366) | This INR study argues that apparent low-frequency architectural bias is partly stable-rank collapse during optimization, and shows that near-orthogonal updates preserve rank and improve signal fidelity. Existing Muon is the rank-preserving intervention rather than a newly invented optimizer, producing gains up to 9 dB PSNR across natural and medical images and novel-view synthesis; however, stable rank does not predict an optimal target, and Muon does not erase architecture gaps. |
| 2025-10-10 | [The Potential of Second-Order Optimization for LLMs: A Study with Full Gauss-Newton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.09378) | Full Gauss-Newton experiments on transformers up to 150M parameters provide an oracle for how much current curvature approximations leave unrealized: GN reaches loss 3.25 in 54 steps versus 292 for SOAP and about 16× more steps for Muon, while layerwise GN needs 78 steps. Muon is both a strong baseline and inner solver, not a new contribution; the roughly 1.5× slower implementation is a diagnostic proof of concept, not yet a practical optimizer. |
| 2025-02-16 | [AdaGC: Enhancing LLM Pretraining Stability via Adaptive Gradient Clipping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11034) | AdaGC clips each tensor's gradient norm relative to an EMA of its historically clipped norms, preventing outliers from contaminating optimizer states with negligible memory and less communication than global clipping. On Llama-2-7B, Mixtral-8×1B, and ERNIE-10B-A1.4B it reduces spike scores to zero and improves accuracy by 1.32, 1.27, and 2.48 points; Muon is only a compatibility test, and AdaGC mitigates symptoms rather than root causes. |

## Muon Blogs, Implementations, and Resources

These resources are not standalone papers. They include project pages, implementation repositories, educational labs, and reference collections that are useful for tracking Muon optimizer practice.

- CSV: [data/muon_resources.csv](./data/muon_resources.csv)
- Inclusion rule: Muon-focused material must provide substantive original documentation, code, experiments, benchmarks, or maintained reference value; empty repositories, straight copies, and incidental mentions are excluded.

| Date | Type | Resource | Notes |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2026-10-15 | official frontier-training report and distributed Muon engineering | [MAI-Base-2: frontier Muon scaling and hyperparameter laws](https://microsoft.ai/news/frontier-pre-training-at-microsoft-ai/) | Microsoft's original MAI-Base-2 report describes full-matrix Muon on 16K GB200 GPUs using local FP32 momentum, BF16 all-to-all resharding, shape-batched ownership, symmetry-aware Newton-Schulz kernels, cached metadata, and communication overlap. The measured optimizer segment is 165 ms, or 2.1% of average iteration time, versus an estimated 55 ms hardware lower bound. Joint learning-rate/decay sweeps motivate empirical step- and tokens-per-parameter scaling laws, while activation-gradient clipping improves stability. The 2.8T-total/80B-active model trains on 50T tokens. These are proprietary-stack engineering and recipe observations; the report's whole-recipe FLOPs gains do not isolate Muon's causal benefit or establish portable hyperparameter laws. |
| 2026-10-13 | original educational implementation-audit lab | [Muon Optimizer: Audit Orthogonal Updates](https://jpcasabianca.com/journal/muon-optimizer-orthogonal-updates/) | Original tutorial and bounded browser lab expose a complete Muon update ledger: semantic parameter routing, momentum and Nesterov state, rectangular orientation, five polynomial iterations, shape scaling, decoupled decay, and final weights. A separate Jacobi-eigensolver polar reference checks the approximation, while five mutation fixtures target misrouted embeddings, missing transposes, wrong multiplication sides, omitted scaling, and coupled decay. Deterministic JSON/CSV receipts retain intermediate hashes and configuration. Examples deliberately use matrices with dimensions two through four and distinguish finite quintic approximations from exact polar factors. This is arithmetic and implementation-integrity education, not a training benchmark, convergence proof, production kernel, or validated hyperparameter recommendation. |
| 2026-10-13 | official distributed optimizer optimization | [TorchTitan DistMuon BF16 input and direction exchange](https://github.com/pytorch/torchtitan/commit/210fc60cb2b88bf21f41ebc65a75a05f254ba99f) [Code](https://github.com/pytorch/torchtitan) | TorchTitan sends redistributed Muon inputs and update directions in BF16, matching the dtype already used by Newton-Schulz while halving exchange buffers. For heterogeneous BlockShard matrices, shape-dependent learning-rate scaling moves to the storage side after transport to avoid an extra BF16 rounding. Reported 40-step checks preserve every Muon parameter and momentum digest, loss, and gradient norm. An eight-A100 Kimi debug run reduces slowest-rank step time from 251.8 to 245.9 ms; an exchange-bound eight-3090 run falls from 2689 to 1195 ms. These short, hardware-specific system measurements establish transport parity and cost savings, not long-horizon optimization or model-quality improvements. |
| 2026-10-10 | official learned-optimizer meta-training implementation | [ELO JAX meta-training and Muon-baseline reproduction suite](https://github.com/xiaol827/ELO) | Paper-linked JAX implementation provides failure-aware resume buffers, progressive expert supervision, persistent-evolution-strategy estimators, and ELO-small_fc/Celo2 configurations. The October 10 material update adds Muon ImageNet baselines, checkpoint averaging and local-checkpoint loading, and JAX sharding compatibility fixes. README recipes cover four tiny meta-training tasks, ImageNet ResNet/ViT evaluation, and GPT-2 language pretraining; separate PyLO links provide PyTorch deployment and pretrained weights. Reproducing runs requires substantial datasets and externally obtained or newly trained checkpoints. This code supports an existing meta-training method, not a new optimizer architecture or proof of frontier-model generalization, and the repository's creation date was May 7. |
| 2026-10-09 | original benchmark blog and reproducibility code | [Comparing Muon, NorMuon and AdamW for Fine-tuning a Dense Retriever](https://qcznlp.github.io/blog/2026/muon-vs-adamw/) [Code](https://github.com/qcznlp/muon-retriever-finetuning) | Original DenseOn retriever fine-tuning study gives each optimizer six learning rates, selects by held-out contrastive loss, and evaluates the chosen recipe over three seeds and fourteen decontaminated BEIR tasks. Muon and NorMuon improve training and validation loss but attain mean nDCG@10 of 59.20 and 59.15 versus AdamW's 59.10, with paired confidence intervals spanning zero. Gains favor in-domain retrieval while scientific and biomedical tasks generally worsen. Code, configurations, result tables, and checkpoints are public. Evidence is one Adam-pretrained encoder, batch size 128, and one epoch; effective weight decay differs with learning rate, and momentum, NS depth, and NorMuon beta2 were not tuned. |
| 2026-10-08 | independent layer-aware Newton-Schulz research implementation | [orthobudget: spectrum-probed cross-layer NS allocation](https://github.com/joelvarun/orthobudget) | Independent MIT-licensed NumPy workbench periodically probes each momentum spectrum, simulates polynomial schedules in scalar arithmetic, and allocates a common FLOP budget across layers through Pareto frontiers and a knapsack solver. On a three-layer synthetic regression task, reported spectral descent efficiency rises from 0.735 to 0.981 at 99.7% of baseline accounted optimizer FLOPs, but five-seed held-out MSE worsens by about 7%. Instrumentation performs additional uncharged eigensolves for measurement; charged FLOPs are not measured GPU throughput. This is original exploratory code with raw results and tests, not an official paper implementation or validated LLM training improvement. |
| 2026-10-06 | original educational optimizer implementation and benchmark | [Optimizers from scratch: NumPy Muon and tuned optimizer race](https://github.com/vukrosic/optimizers-from-scratch) | Original MIT-licensed NumPy explanations implement gradient descent, SGD, momentum, RMSProp, Adam, AdamW, and Muon alongside a hand-written network and backward pass, deterministic tests, and saved demonstration output. A 425-run race tunes five optimizers over 17 learning rates and five initializations; Muon reaches median test-digit accuracy of 96.0% versus Adam's 94.6%. The experiment uses only a 6,634-parameter dense network and 8-by-8 handwritten digits, fixed learning rates, and Adam fallback for biases. It is useful for understanding update rules and controlled comparisons, not evidence for language-model scaling or a new optimizer paper. |
| 2026-10-04 | official operator-aware implementation and validation | [Megatron-LM per-head Muon and packed-MLA dev integration](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/pull/7085) [Code](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/commit/f80139c3efc3c0a03c9669df5db5e310f68ad4c7) | Merged Megatron-LM dev integration adds opt-in per-head Q/gate/K/V orthogonalization and module-owned logical layouts for MLA up-projections. Equal-size heads use batched Newton-Schulz; tensor-parallel fragments are reconstructed before logical splitting, fixing incorrect boundaries when TP exceeds query-group count. MLA splitting requires a separate opt-in, while per-head or fragmented layouts fall back from distributed NS. Numerical and multi-GPU regression tests accompany 100-step mock-data runs: per-head overhead varies from roughly zero to 1.4%, and combined FP8 parameter gathering lowers peak memory about 7%. The memory gain belongs to FP8 gathering; short nondeterministic mock-data runs do not establish better convergence or downstream quality. |
| 2026-09-30 | official optimizer implementation and reproduction suite | [AF-Muon optimizer and tied-embedding training code](https://github.com/arashlagzian/afmoun) | Paper-linked repository implementing hidden-matrix Muon, one-dimensional normalized momentum, and a chunked support-aware finite-cap oracle for tied embeddings/heads. It includes nine benchmark configurations, launchers, parameter-role mapping, and ablation scripts. The repository does not bundle training datasets, caches, or pretrained weights; published memory savings concern optimizer state rather than total device memory. |
| 2026-09-28 | official distributed optimizer follow-up study | [LionMuon distributed exact-gradient study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35297) [Code](https://github.com/brain-lab-research/lion-muon) | New submission studying the already-listed LionMuon algorithm under four-GPU exact-gradient DDP, not a distinct optimizer. Period-5 LionMuon reaches the 124M Muon target loss with 0.86× steps, 0.79× update FLOPs, 0.69× communicated bytes, and 0.70× PCIe or 0.77× NVLink time. Evidence is three seeds at 124M and one at 355M; alternation-period hyperparameters need retuning and sharded baselines are evaluated in a DDP/emulated setting. |
| 2026-09-28 | official Muon dynamics experiment code | [Muon split edge-of-stability diagnostics](https://github.com/cyzebra/Muon-Sublates-the-Edge-of-Stability-in-LLM-Pretraining) | Paper-linked training and conditional-loss/alignment diagnostics for 22M, 130M, and 1B language models plus small-network controls, with momentum-free NS-5 Muon and separate AdamW parameter routing. Reference-loss probes and exact-SVD nuclear-norm diagnostics add substantial evaluation cost; the scripts train on one device and no result plots or reports are bundled. |
| 2026-09-28 | educational interactive lab | [Muon, Part 2: Newton–Schulz Without the Mystery](https://neelmishra.github.io/blog/dl/optimizers/newton-schulz.html) [Code](https://github.com/neelmishra/neelmishra.github.io/blob/main/blog/dl/optimizers/newton-schulz.html) | Original browser-based matrix and singular-value animations compare classical cubic polar iteration with Muon's fixed quintic, demonstrating normalization, weak-direction growth, rank-deficient partial-polar behavior, tall-matrix transposition, and non-monotone quintic error; the double-precision 2×2 labs illustrate inner transforms rather than BF16 or full-model benchmarks. |
| 2026-09-27 | official Muon-family speedrun method and ablation report | [Training NanoGPT in 39.9 Seconds: ANVIL II](https://hyperstition.cc/training-nanogpt-in-39-9-seconds) [Code](https://github.com/KellerJordan/modded-nanogpt/tree/f9b6266f0b0aa3f4316b86394bce9304c84afdf8) | Author-written report explains ANVIL II's fast and slow momentum streams, six spectral polynomial maps, cautious decay, and late weight averaging, with public training code and measurements in modded-nanogpt PR 360. The complete 8-H100 trainer reaches the 3.28 validation target in 39.914 seconds versus 73.889 for record 89, across 18 and nine runs respectively. It derives the small-singular-value correspondence between iteration count and effective regularization, then proposes adding an explicit diagonal term inside Gram Newton-Schulz. The note also explains how Frobenius normalization rescales that regularization parameter. It is exploratory literature, not a peer-reviewed optimizer evaluation: the authors report no compelling evidence that tuning the added regularization improves training, with preliminary experiments often favoring the exact polar map. Possible reinforcement-learning and fine-tuning benefits remain noisy, and improved numerical stability is explicitly untested. |
